    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "prof:s-expansion",
        "exact_quote": "Let $p$ be the matched solution above. There are uniquely determined coefficients $d_j$, extending <a href=\"#prof:asymptotic-polynomial\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:asymptotic-polynomial\">[prof:asymptotic-polynomial]</a>, such that for every $J,\\ell\\ge0$, $$\\label{prof:s-expansion}\n \\frac{\\mathrm{d}^\\ell}{\\mathrm{d}s^\\ell}\n \\left(p(s)-\\sum_{j=1}^{J}d_js^{-3j}\\right)\n =O\\bigl(s^{-3(J+1)-\\ell}\\bigr).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-352.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-prof-weighted-remainder",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-prof-weighted-remainder</summary>\n\n*Proof.* The limits $p,p',p''\\to0$ and <a href=\"#prof:ode\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:ode\">[prof:ode]</a> imply that every derivative of $p$ is bounded on a sufficiently late half-line. To see this without assuming any asymptotic expansion, solve <a href=\"#prof:ode\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:ode\">[prof:ode]</a> for $p'''$. Its right-hand side is bounded, since $p$ is separated from $-1$. Successively differentiating this formula proves the claim by induction: the coefficient functions $s^{-j}$ have bounded derivatives and all derivatives of $p\\mapsto p/(1+p)$ are bounded on the range in question.\n\n\nFormal substitution of $p=\\sum_{j\\ge1}d_jt^j$, $t=s^{-3}$, in <a href=\"#prof:ode\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:ode\">[prof:ode]</a> determines the coefficients successively. Indeed the coefficient of $t^j$ contains the new coefficient $d_j$ with coefficient one from $p/(1+p)$; every other term involves only previous coefficients. The source $-Cd/s^3$ determines $d_1=Cd$. For $p_J=\\sum_{j=1}^Jd_js^{-3j}$, including $p_0=0$, one consequently has $$\\label{prof:formal-residual}\n e_J:=\\mathcal E_d(p_J),\\qquad\n e_J^{(\\ell)}(s)=O\\bigl(s^{-N-\\ell}\\bigr),\n \\quad N=3(J+1),\\quad \\ell\\ge0.$$ This follows directly from analyticity of the finite expression in $s^{-3}$ near zero; no convergence of the infinite formal series is asserted.\n\n\nLet $W=p-p_J$. The exact divided-difference identity $$\\frac p{1+p}-\\frac{p_J}{1+p_J}\n =\\frac{W}{(1+p)(1+p_J)}$$ gives the linear equation $$\\label{prof:remainder-equation}\n W'''+W=-e_J-a_2W''-a_1W'-a_0W,$$ where $$a_2=\\frac{30}{s},\\qquad a_1=\\frac B{s^2},\\qquad\n a_0=\\frac C{s^3}+\\frac1{(1+p)(1+p_J)}-1.$$ These are now treated as given coefficient functions. Their absolute sum tends to zero because $p,p_J\\to0$, and all their derivatives are bounded on a sufficiently late half-line.\n\n\n\nThe polynomial $\\lambda^3+1$ has roots $-1$ and $(1\\pm i\\sqrt3)/2$. The analogue of <a href=\"#prof:green-formula\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:green-formula\">[prof:green-formula]</a> on $[S,\\infty)$, with these roots, writes the bounded solution of $W'''+W=j$ as a stable homogeneous vector plus a convolution vector $\\mathcal K_Sj$. Every root has modulus one and $|(\\lambda^3+1)'|=3$ at the roots. Hence $$\\label{prof:weighted-green}\n \\max_{0\\le i\\le2}\\lVert (\\mathcal K_Sj)_i\\rVert_\\infty\n \\le\\frac53\\lVert j\\rVert_\\infty.$$ It also acts in the Banach space with norm $\\lVert j\\rVert_N=\\sup_{s\\ge S}s^N|j(s)|$. For $S\\le u\\le s$, $$(s/u)^N\\le\\exp\\bigl(N(s-u)/S\\bigr),$$ while for $u\\ge s$ this ratio is at most one. If $S\\ge2N$, the forward kernel is therefore bounded by an exponential with decay rate $1/2$, and the two backward kernels already have decay rate $1/2$. It follows that $$\\label{prof:weighted-green-2}\n \\max_{0\\le i\\le2}\\sup_{s\\ge S}s^N| (\\mathcal K_Sj)_i(s)|\n \\le2\\lVert j\\rVert_N.$$ The stable homogeneous vector $H(s)=q_S\\mathrm{e}^{-(s-S)}(1,-1,1)^t$ belongs to both spaces. Here $q_S$ is chosen to be the stable coordinate of the actual jet $(W,W',W'')(S)$.\n\n\nChoose $S\\ge2N$ so late that $2\\sup_{s\\ge S}(|a_0|+|a_1|+|a_2|)<1$. The affine equation for the source, $$\\label{prof:weighted-source}\n j=-e_J-\\sum_{i=0}^2a_i\\bigl(H_i+(\\mathcal K_Sj)_i\\bigr),$$ is a contraction both in the bounded norm and in the norm $\\lVert \\cdot\\rVert_N$, by <a href=\"#prof:weighted-green\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:weighted-green\">[prof:weighted-green]</a>–<a href=\"#prof:weighted-green-2\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:weighted-green-2\">[prof:weighted-green-2]</a>. It has a unique solution in each space. The weighted solution is bounded, so uniqueness in the bounded space identifies the two solutions. On the other hand, the actual remainder has a bounded jet and bounded source $j=W'''+W$. The growing-mode integrals from infinity are forced by boundedness, so its jet has the representation $H+\\mathcal K_Sj$ with the chosen $q_S$. It satisfies <a href=\"#prof:weighted-source\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:weighted-source\">[prof:weighted-source]</a> and hence equals the weighted solution. We have proved $$\\label{prof:preliminary-remainder}\n W,W',W''=O(s^{-N}).$$\n\nEquation <a href=\"#prof:remainder-equation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:remainder-equation\">[prof:remainder-equation]</a> gives $W'''=O(s^{-N})$. Differentiating it repeatedly, using the bounded coefficient derivatives and <a href=\"#prof:formal-residual\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:formal-residual\">[prof:formal-residual]</a>, proves $W^{(\\ell)}=O(s^{-N})$ for every fixed $\\ell$. To obtain the extra decay for each derivative in <a href=\"#prof:s-expansion\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:s-expansion\">[prof:s-expansion]</a>, fix $J,\\ell$ and apply this last conclusion to the deeper truncation $p_K$, where $K\\ge J+\\ell$. Then $$(p-p_J)^{(\\ell)}\n =(p-p_K)^{(\\ell)}+\n   \\sum_{j=J+1}^{K}d_j(s^{-3j})^{(\\ell)}\n =O\\bigl(s^{-3(J+1)-\\ell}\\bigr).$$ The starting point $S$ and constants may depend on the finite orders $J,\\ell$, which is exactly what the asserted asymptotic statement requires. ◻",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $p$ be the matched solution above. There are uniquely determined coefficients $d_j$, extending <a href=\"#prof:asymptotic-polynomial\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:asymptotic-polynomial\">[prof:asymptotic-polynomial]</a>, such that for every $J,\\ell\\ge0$, $$\\label{prof:s-expansion}\n \\frac{\\mathrm{d}^\\ell}{\\mathrm{d}s^\\ell}\n \\left(p(s)-\\sum_{j=1}^{J}d_js^{-3j}\\right)\n =O\\bigl(s^{-3(J+1)-\\ell}\\bigr).$$",
      "explanation": "The matched solution has asymptotic expansions with differentiated remainder estimates at every fixed order. Uniqueness among bounded remainders identifies the actual solution with the solution constructed in the required polynomial weight.",

      "kind": "bound",

          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        },

          "anchor": "stmt-prof-weighted-remainder",
          "exact_quote": "such that for every $J,\\ell\\ge0$,",

        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-prof-weighted-remainder"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ana-schauder",
        "exact_quote": "Under these assumptions the following estimates hold.*\n\n1.  *If $Q_R=B_R\\times(-R^4,0]$ and $\\mathcal L u=f$ on $Q_2$, then $$\\label{ana:interior-schauder}\n     \\mathcal N_\\alpha(u;Q_1)\n     \\le C\\bigl(|f|_{\\alpha;Q_2}+\\|u\\|_{\\infty;Q_2}\\bigr).$$*\n\n\n2.  *If $0<h\\le1$, $\\mathcal L u=f$ on $B_2\\times[0,h]$, and $u(\\cdot,0)=0$, then $$\\label{ana:initial-schauder}\n     \\mathcal N_\\alpha(u;B_1\\times[0,h])\n     \\le C\\bigl(|f|_{\\alpha;B_2\\times[0,h]}\n                       +\\|u\\|_{\\infty;B_2\\times[0,h]}\\bigr).$$ Here $C$ depends only on $d,\\alpha,\\lambda,K$ and the fixed spatial gaps, and in <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span> is independent of $h$.*\n\n*These are a priori estimates for classical solutions in the indicated spaces; smooth approximation gives the same conclusions for limits of such solutions. The usual interior regularity theorem also applies: with smooth coefficients, distributional solutions in $L^2_{\\rm loc}$ and Hölder sources are locally in the space <a href=\"#ana:local-norm\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:local-norm\">[ana:local-norm]</a>, and are smooth when the source is smooth. The estimates are from preceding times and require no terminal data.",
        "source_id": "PM-352.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-ana-schauder",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-ana-schauder</summary>\n\n\n*Proof.* We give the kernel and localization argument, particularly its uniformity in <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span>. For a frozen homogeneous principal operator $A_0$, with symbol $p(\\xi)\\ge\\lambda|\\xi|^4$, its heat kernel is $$k_b(z)=(2\\pi)^{-d}\\int_{\\mathbb{R}^d}e^{iz\\cdot\\xi-bp(\\xi)}\\,\\mathrm{d}\\xi,\n \\qquad b>0.$$ Fourier differentiation and scaling give, uniformly for the allowed symbols and every $N,j,\\ell$, $$\\label{ana:kernel}\n |D^j\\partial_b^\\ell k_b(z)|\n \\le C_{N,j,\\ell}b^{-(d+j)/4-\\ell}\n                  (1+|z|b^{-1/4})^{-N}.$$ Moreover $\\int k_b=1$ and $\\int D^jk_b=0$ for $j>0$. For $u(t)=\\int_0^tk_{t-s}*f(s)\\,\\mathrm{d}s$ and $j=4$, subtract $f(s,z)$ inside the convolution. Writing $F=\\sup_s[f(s)]_{\\alpha;\\mathbb{R}^d}$, $$\\label{ana:kernel-moment}\n \\|D^4k_b*f(s)\\|_\\infty\\le CF b^{-1+\\alpha/4},\\qquad\n \\|\\partial_bD^4k_b*f(s)\\|_\\infty\n                    \\le CF b^{-2+\\alpha/4}.$$ This proves $\\|D^4u(t)\\|_\\infty\\le CFt^{\\alpha/4}$. For a spatial increment of size $q$, split the lag integral at $q^4$. The short-lag part is bounded by $CF\\int_0^{q^4}b^{-1+\\alpha/4}\\,\\mathrm{d}b$. For the long-lag part use the mean value theorem and $\\|D^5k_b*f(s)\\|_\\infty\\le CFb^{(\\alpha-5)/4}$. Its bound is $CFq\\int_{q^4}^h b^{(\\alpha-5)/4}\\,\\mathrm{d}b$. Both are at most $CFq^\\alpha$, with an empty integral omitted.\n\nFor a temporal increment $e=t-s>0$, keep the source time unchanged: $$\\begin{aligned}\n D^4u(t)-D^4u(s)\n ={}&\\int_s^tD^4k_{t-r}*f(r)\\,\\mathrm{d}r\\\\\n &+\\int_0^s(D^4k_{t-r}-D^4k_{s-r})*f(r)\\,\\mathrm{d}r.\n\\end{aligned}$$ The first integral and the part of the second where $s-r<e$ are bounded by $CF e^{\\alpha/4}$. On the remaining part, <a href=\"#ana:kernel-moment\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:kernel-moment\">[ana:kernel-moment]</a> bounds the difference by $CF e(s-r)^{-2+\\alpha/4}$, and $$e\\int_e^s b^{-2+\\alpha/4}\\,\\mathrm{d}b\\le C e^{\\alpha/4}.$$ This proves the full parabolic $\\alpha$ bound for $D^4u$, down to the initial face, without any requirement that $f(0)$ vanish. The bounds for $u$ and lower derivatives follow from the kernel integrals or <a href=\"#ana:interp-basic\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:interp-basic\">[ana:interp-basic]</a>; finally $u_t=f-A_0u$ supplies the full parabolic norm of $u_t$. We have proved the constant-coefficient zero-data estimate on every slab of length at most one, with one constant.\n\n\nHere are the localization details needed to preserve that constant. We apply the fourth-derivative frozen estimate in the original local units before localization; its constant is independent of the localization radius. In a spatial ball of radius $q$ and a time window of length at most $q^4$, freeze the principal coefficients at one point. Use a spatial cutoff supported in that ball. For windows meeting the initial face use no lower-time cutoff, so the localized function still has zero initial value. For interior windows insert a time cutoff which vanishes at the bottom and equals one on the smaller window; its derivatives are bounded by fixed powers of $q^{-1}$. The constant-coefficient estimate then applies in both cases. The localized forcing consists of the original source, terms of spatial order at most three, a time-cutoff multiple of $u$, and $(a_\\nu^0-a_\\nu)D^\\nu u$ for $|\\nu|=4$. The last terms obey $$\\label{ana:freeze-bound}\n |(a_\\nu^0-a_\\nu)D^\\nu u|_\\alpha\n \\le CKq^\\alpha|D^4u|_\\alpha\n             +CK\\|D^4u\\|_\\infty,$$ with fixed cutoff factors understood. First make $q$ small enough to absorb the first coefficient. Apply <a href=\"#ana:fourth-sup-interp\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:fourth-sup-interp\">[ana:fourth-sup-interp]</a> to the second term. For the lower-order terms apply <a href=\"#ana:interp-basic\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:interp-basic\">[ana:interp-basic]</a>, and use the equation only in the form $$\\label{ana:ut-sup}\n \\|u_t\\|_\\infty\\le\\|f\\|_\\infty\n                   +K\\sum_{j=0}^4\\|D^ju\\|_\\infty.$$ Thus no unknown temporal Hölder seminorm of $u_t$ is used to absorb a temporal seminorm of $D^4u$.\n\nFor completeness, the resulting nested-domain absorption can be expressed as follows. Let $H(r)$ be the supremum and parabolic $\\alpha$ norm of $D^4u$ on the spatial ball of radius $r$, with the given time interval in the initial case; in the interior case also increase the preceding-time window with $r$. For $1\\le r<R\\le3/2$, localization, interpolation, and covering give, for any fixed $\\vartheta>0$, $$H(r)\\le\\vartheta H(R)\n       +C_\\vartheta(R-r)^{-k}\n                      (|f|_\\alpha+\\|u\\|_\\infty).$$ Here $k$ is fixed; all losses are powers of spatial cutoff radii, and none is a power of $h^{-1}$. For Hölder pairs not sharing a small localization window, the quotient is bounded by the corresponding inverse power of that window’s radius times $\\|D^4u\\|_\\infty$, which is handled by <a href=\"#ana:fourth-sup-interp\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:fourth-sup-interp\">[ana:fourth-sup-interp]</a>. Iterate along radii increasing geometrically to $3/2$, choosing $\\vartheta 2^k<1$. The last term tends to zero since the solution norm is finite on that compact cylinder. This proves the estimate for $D^4u$. Equations <a href=\"#ana:interp-basic\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:interp-basic\">[ana:interp-basic]</a> and <a href=\"#ana:ut-sup\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:ut-sup\">[ana:ut-sup]</a> then give the lower derivatives, and the equation in full Hölder norm gives $|u_t|_\\alpha$. This proves <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span> and <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span>.\n\nFor the distributional regularity assertion, extend the coefficients and a spatially localized source from a compact coordinate cylinder to a flat torus, combining the principal symbol with $|\\xi|^4$ outside the coordinate patch. This convex combination preserves positivity. The closed-manifold Cauchy theorem (Huang 2026, Theorem 2.4) [[Huang2026](#ref-Huang2026)], with order $r=4$ and zero data at an earlier time, provides a particular solution $w\\in C^{4+\\alpha,1+\\alpha/4}$ near the smaller cylinder. There $u-w$ is a homogeneous distributional solution. To apply (Hörmander 1961, Theorem 5.1) [[Hormander1961](#ref-Hormander1961)], write the full symbol as $p=i\\tau+a_4(x,t,\\xi)+a_{\\le3}(x,t,\\xi)$ and set $R=1+|\\tau|+|\\xi|^4$. On a compact coordinate set, ellipticity and lower-order absorption give $|p|\\ge cR$ at large frequencies. Smoothness and polynomial degree then give $$|\\partial_{x,t}^{B}\\partial_{\\xi,\\tau}^{A}p|\n \\le C_{A,B}(1+|\\xi|+|\\tau|)^{-|A|/4}|p|.$$ This verifies condition HE in (Hörmander 1961, Equations (3.1)’ and (3.2)) [[Hormander1961](#ref-Hormander1961)] with $d=1/4$ and $M_j=1$. Bounded frequencies are covered by $\\widetilde p=(\\sum_A|\\partial_{\\xi,\\tau}^Ap|^2)^{1/2}\n\\ge|\\partial_\\tau p|=1$. Thus $u-w$ is smooth, proving local Hölder regularity. For smooth forcing the same theorem directly gives smoothness of $u$.\n\nThe fixed-cylinder Schauder estimate, including an $L^2$ input for the solution, can also be taken directly from (Dong and Zhang 2015, Theorem 2.2<span class=\"upright\">(I)</span>) [[DongZhang2015](#ref-DongZhang2015)]. Its estimate for the top spatial derivatives is parabolic; the full temporal norm of $u_t$ follows here from the equation and the assumed temporal Hölder regularity of the coefficients and source. This invocation concerns fixed interior cylinders; the uniform initial-face assertion was proved above. ◻",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"

The usual interior regularity theorem also applies: with smooth coefficients, distributional solutions in $L^2_{\\rm loc}$ and Hölder sources are locally in the space <a href=\"#ana:local-norm\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:local-norm\">[ana:local-norm]</a>, and are smooth when the source is smooth. The estimates are from preceding times and require no terminal data.",
      "explanation": "Uniformly elliptic scalar parabolic operators admit interior estimates and zero-data initial-face estimates in the full local parabolic norm. The initial-face constant is independent of the elapsed time, and no terminal data are required.",

      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "ana:operator",
          "exact_quote": "Consider in a coordinate cylinder the real scalar operator $$\\label{ana:operator}\n \\mathcal L u=u_t+\\sum_{|\\nu|\\le4}a_\\nu(z,t)D^\\nu u,\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{|\\nu|=4}a_\\nu(z,t)\\xi^\\nu\\ge\\lambda|\\xi|^4.$$ Assume $\\lambda>0$ and $\\sum_{|\\nu|\\le4}|a_\\nu|_\\alpha\\le K$. These assumptions are always imposed in the coordinates and units of the estimate. On a fixed smooth manifold they include the coefficients arising from the coordinate expression of the operator. The leading symbols used later are positive multiples of squared positive definite quadratic forms, and satisfy <a href=\"#ana:operator\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:operator\">[ana:operator]</a>.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "ana:l2-estimate",
        "exact_quote": "Let $U_1\\Subset U_2$ be fixed bounded spatial domains and $b_0<b_1<b$, with $b-b_1$ bounded above. Suppose that the coefficient assumptions of Lemma <a href=\"#ana:schauder\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ana:schauder\">13</a> hold on $U_2\\times(b_0,b)$ and that $\\mathcal Lu=f$ there. Then $$\\label{ana:l2-estimate}\n \\mathcal N_\\alpha(u;U_1\\times[b_1,b))\n \\le C\\bigl(|f|_{\\alpha;U_2\\times(b_0,b)}\n                  +\\|u\\|_{L^2(U_2\\times(b_0,b))}\\bigr).$$ The constant depends on the spatial gap, $b_1-b_0$, the upper bound for $b-b_1$, and the coefficient constants, but requires no terminal trace. The estimate is unchanged if the upper face is included and the solution is defined there. In particular it holds on fixed nested annuli through an open upper time endpoint.",
        "source_id": "PM-352.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-ana-l2",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-ana-l2</summary>\n\n*Proof.* We recall an interpolation proof of the replacement of the supremum term. On three nested interior cylinders, with a gap of parabolic size $a$, the estimate already proved bounds $\\|D_zu\\|_\\infty$ and $\\|u_t\\|_\\infty$ on the middle cylinder by $Ca^{-k}(\\|u\\|_{\\infty;\\mathrm{outer}}+|f|_\\alpha)$. Average $u$ on a preceding cylinder of radius $q<a/4$ ending at the point being estimated. Its volume is a fixed multiple of $q^{d+4}$, so $$|u(z,t)|\\le Cq^{-(d+4)/2}\\|u\\|_{L^2(\\mathrm{outer})}\n           +C(q+q^4)a^{-k}\n                    (\\|u\\|_{\\infty;\\mathrm{outer}}+|f|_\\alpha).$$ Choose $q$ to be a sufficiently small fixed multiple of a suitable power of $a$. The last supremum coefficient can be made any prescribed $\\vartheta>0$, at the cost of another fixed inverse power of $a$ on the other terms. Iteration over geometrically decreasing gaps, with $\\vartheta$ smaller than the corresponding geometric growth factor, bounds the inner supremum by $C(\\|u\\|_{L^2}+|f|_\\alpha)$. Lemma <a href=\"#ana:schauder\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ana:schauder\">13</a> on one further nested cylinder proves <a href=\"#ana:l2-estimate\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:l2-estimate\">[ana:l2-estimate]</a>; finite covering handles $U_1$ and the time interval. All averaging cylinders use preceding times. First carry this out up to any closed upper face $b'<b$ on which the local norms are finite, then let $b'\\uparrow b$. The constants use only the fixed lower time buffer. Pairs of points approaching $b$ are included by this limiting argument. ◻",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }

Then $$\\label{ana:l2-estimate}\n \\mathcal N_\\alpha(u;U_1\\times[b_1,b))\n \\le C\\bigl(|f|_{\\alpha;U_2\\times(b_0,b)}\n                  +\\|u\\|_{L^2(U_2\\times(b_0,b))}\\bigr).$$ The constant depends on the spatial gap, $b_1-b_0$, the upper bound for $b-b_1$, and the coefficient constants, but requires no terminal trace.         "anchor": "ana:short-bounds",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $p\\in\\mathbb{R}$, $0<\\alpha<1$, and the preceding scale and coefficient constants. There exist $\\eta_0>0$ and $C<\\infty$, independent of $\\inf s$, with the following property. If $$0<T\\le\\eta_0(\\inf s)^4,\\qquad\n \\mathcal Lu=f\\quad\\hbox{on }M\\times[0,T],\\qquad u(\\cdot,0)=0,$$ then, with $\\eta=T/(\\inf s)^4$, $$\\label{ana:short-bounds}\n \\|u\\|_{X_{\\alpha,p}(s)}\\le C\\|f\\|_{Y_{\\alpha,p}(s)},\\qquad\n \\sup_{z,t}s(z)^{-p}|u(z,t)|\n                         \\le C\\eta\\|f\\|_{Y_{\\alpha,p}(s)}.$$ For each fixed positive scale and smooth coefficients the zero-data Cauchy problem has a unique solution with this regularity for $f\\in Y_{\\alpha,p}(s)$. The same estimates hold on every interval $[0,T')$, $T'\\le T$, on which the coefficients and source obey the stated bounds.",
        "source_id": "PM-352.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-ana-short",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-ana-short</summary>\n\n*Proof.* Choose $\\eta_0<a_0^4$. Every defining box meets the initial face, and its full time interval has length at most $\\eta_0$ in center-scale units. Apply <a href=\"#ana:initial-schauder\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:initial-schauder\">[ana:initial-schauder]</a> on a fixed spatial enlargement of each such box, rescaling its fixed spatial radius if necessary. Scale comparability and the covering observation after Definition <a href=\"#ana:spaces\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ana:spaces\">12</a> give $$\\|u\\|_{X_{\\alpha,p}(s)}\n \\le C\\bigl(\\|f\\|_{Y_{\\alpha,p}(s)}+S\\bigr),\\qquad\n S=\\sup_{z,t}s(z)^{-p}|u(z,t)|.$$ The pointwise time-derivative bound in this norm and zero initial data imply $$S\\le\\sup_z\\frac{T}{s(z)^4}\\|u\\|_{X_{\\alpha,p}(s)}\n   \\le\\eta\\|u\\|_{X_{\\alpha,p}(s)}.$$ Decrease $\\eta_0$ so that $C\\eta_0\\le1/2$, and absorb. This proves both estimates in <a href=\"#ana:short-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:short-bounds\">[ana:short-bounds]</a> with constants independent of the number or size of the spatial boxes.\n\nFixed-scale existence and uniqueness follow from (Huang 2026, Theorem 2.4) [[Huang2026](#ref-Huang2026)], with the trivial real line bundle, $r=4$, exponent $\\alpha$, $L_t=-A(t)$, and zero initial data, where $\\mathcal L=\\partial_t+A(t)$. Its solution is $C^{4+\\alpha,1+\\alpha/4}$ on the closed time slab, without requiring $f(\\cdot,0)=0$; the equation gives $u_t(\\cdot,0)=f(\\cdot,0)$. Constants in this fixed-scale existence argument need not be uniform in $\\inf s$; uniformity of the asserted estimates has already been established locally. Truncation before an open terminal face and passage to the limit prove the last statement. ◻",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Fix $p\\in\\mathbb{R}$, $0<\\alpha<1$, and the preceding scale and coefficient constants. The same estimates hold on every interval $[0,T')$, $T'\\le T$, on which the coefficients and source obey the stated bounds.",

        {

        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-19",
          "exact_quote": "#### The outer forward problem\n\nThe following formulation permits the localization scale to tend to zero. Fix a compact smooth manifold without boundary and a time-independent positive Lipschitz scale $s(z)$. Assume that on balls of radius $4a_0s(z)$ the scale is comparable to its center value, with uniform comparison constants and uniformly bounded coordinate changes in scaled units. We impose the coefficient assumptions of <a href=\"#ana:operator\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:operator\">[ana:operator]</a> uniformly after scaling space by $s(z)$ and time by $s(z)^4$; an original coefficient of spatial order $j$ is thus multiplied by $s(z)^{4-j}$. The ellipticity and Hölder constants in these units are denoted again by $\\lambda,K$. These hypotheses hold for $s=s_\\beta$ in the later application.\n\n\n<div id=\"ana:short\" class=\"lemma\">\n\n\n**Lemma 15** (Uniform zero-data forward estimate). *Fix $p\\in\\mathbb{R}$, $0<\\alpha<1$, and the preceding scale and coefficient constants. There exist $\\eta_0>0$ and $C<\\infty$, independent of $\\inf s$, with the following property. If $$0<T\\le\\eta_0(\\inf s)^4,\\qquad\n \\mathcal Lu=f\\quad\\hbox{on }M\\times[0,T],\\qquad u(\\cdot,0)=0,$$ then, with $\\eta=T/(\\inf s)^4$, $$\\label{ana:short-bounds}\n \\|u\\|_{X_{\\alpha,p}(s)}\\le C\\|f\\|_{Y_{\\alpha,p}(s)},\\qquad\n \\sup_{z,t}s(z)^{-p}|u(z,t)|\n                         \\le C\\eta\\|f\\|_{Y_{\\alpha,p}(s)}.$$ For each fixed positive scale and smooth coefficients the zero-data Cauchy problem has a unique solution with this regularity for $f\\in Y_{\\alpha,p}(s)$. The same estimates hold on every interval $[0,T')$, $T'\\le T$, on which the coefficients and source obey the stated bounds.*\n\n</div>\n\n<details id=\"proof-stmt-ana-short\"><summary>Proof of stmt-ana-short</summary>\n\n*Proof.* Choose $\\eta_0<a_0^4$. Every defining box meets the initial face, and its full time interval has length at most $\\eta_0$ in center-scale units. Apply <a href=\"#ana:initial-schauder\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:initial-schauder\">[ana:initial-schauder]</a> on a fixed spatial enlargement of each such box, rescaling its fixed spatial radius if necessary. Scale comparability and the covering observation after Definition <a href=\"#ana:spaces\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ana:spaces\">12</a> give $$\\|u\\|_{X_{\\alpha,p}(s)}\n \\le C\\bigl(\\|f\\|_{Y_{\\alpha,p}(s)}+S\\bigr),\\qquad\n S=\\sup_{z,t}s(z)^{-p}|u(z,t)|.$$ The pointwise time-derivative bound in this norm and zero initial data imply $$S\\le\\sup_z\\frac{T}{s(z)^4}\\|u\\|_{X_{\\alpha,p}(s)}\n   \\le\\eta\\|u\\|_{X_{\\alpha,p}(s)}.$$ Decrease $\\eta_0$ so that $C\\eta_0\\le1/2$, and absorb. This proves both estimates in <a href=\"#ana:short-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:short-bounds\">[ana:short-bounds]</a> with constants independent of the number or size of the spatial boxes.\n\nFixed-scale existence and uniqueness follow from (Huang 2026, Theorem 2.4) [[Huang2026](#ref-Huang2026)], with the trivial real line bundle, $r=4$, exponent $\\alpha$, $L_t=-A(t)$, and zero initial data, where $\\mathcal L=\\partial_t+A(t)$. Its solution is $C^{4+\\alpha,1+\\alpha/4}$ on the closed time slab, without requiring $f(\\cdot,0)=0$; the equation gives $u_t(\\cdot,0)=f(\\cdot,0)$. Constants in this fixed-scale existence argument need not be uniform in $\\inf s$; uniformity of the asserted estimates has already been established locally. Truncation before an open terminal face and passage to the limit prove the last statement. ◻\n\n</details>\n\n\n<div id=\"ana:interpolation\" class=\"lemma\">\n\n\n\n**Lemma 16** (Small spatial derivatives on a short interval). *Under the hypotheses of Lemma <a href=\"#ana:short\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ana:short\">15</a>, the local scaled parabolic $C^\\alpha$ norms of the spatial derivatives of order $0\\le j\\le3$, with weight $p-j$, satisfy $$\\label{ana:lower-small}\n \\sup_P\\bigl|D_y^j(s_0^{-p}u\\circ\\Psi_P)\\bigr|_{\\alpha;D_P}\n \\le C\\eta^{(4-j-\\alpha)/4}\\|f\\|_{Y_{\\alpha,p}(s)}.$$ If the coefficient and source bounds also hold at an exponent $\\alpha'\\in(\\alpha,1)$, then $$\\label{ana:fourth-small}\n \\|u\\|_{X'_{\\alpha,p}(s)}\n \\le C\\eta^{(\\alpha'-\\alpha)/4}\n                    \\|f\\|_{Y_{\\alpha',p}(s)}.$$ Constants may depend on $\\alpha'$ but remain independent of the scale and elapsed time.*\n\n</div>\n\n<details id=\"proof-stmt-ana-interpolation\"><summary>Proof of stmt-ana-interpolation</summary>\n\n*Proof.* On a fixed enlarged scaled box, <a href=\"#ana:short-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:short-bounds\">[ana:short-bounds]</a> gives $U\\le C\\eta\\|f\\|_Y$ and $M\\le C\\|f\\|_Y$ in the notation of <a href=\"#ana:interp-basic\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:interp-basic\">[ana:interp-basic]</a>. That inequality proves <a href=\"#ana:lower-small\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:lower-small\">[ana:lower-small]</a>. In particular, all derivatives entering a commutator of a fourth-order spatial operator with a spatial cutoff have a common sufficient small factor $\\eta^{(1-\\alpha)/4}$.\n\nFor the second claim apply Lemma <a href=\"#ana:short\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ana:short\">15</a> at exponent $\\alpha'$, decreasing $\\eta_0$ if necessary. Every spatial derivative of $u$ through order four has zero initial trace. Indeed the solution and these derivatives are continuous to the initial face, and the trace function $u(\\cdot,0)$ is identically zero. For each $g=D_y^j(s_0^{-p}u\\circ\\Psi_P)$, $0\\le j\\le4$, the temporal $\\alpha'/4$ seminorm and the elapsed scaled time therefore give $$\\|g\\|_\\infty\\le C\\eta^{\\alpha'/4}\n                            \\|f\\|_{Y_{\\alpha',p}(s)},\\qquad\n [g]_{\\alpha'}\\le C\\|f\\|_{Y_{\\alpha',p}(s)}.$$ On any metric space, splitting distances where the estimates $|g(P)-g(Q)|\\le2\\|g\\|_\\infty$ and $|g(P)-g(Q)|\\le[g]_{\\alpha'}d(P,Q)^{\\alpha'}$ agree proves $$[g]_\\alpha\\le\n C\\|g\\|_\\infty^{1-\\alpha/\\alpha'}\n                   [g]_{\\alpha'}^{\\alpha/\\alpha'}.$$ Apply this with the parabolic metric and take the supremum over boxes. It proves <a href=\"#ana:fourth-small\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:fourth-small\">[ana:fourth-small]</a>, including temporal Hölder quotients of $D^4u$ with one point at the initial face. ◻\n\n</details>\n\n\n<div id=\"ana:no-time-smallness\" class=\"remark\">\n\n*Remark 17*. The exponent gap and omission of $u_t$ in <a href=\"#ana:fourth-small\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:fourth-small\">[ana:fourth-small]</a> are necessary. On a circle the exact zero-data solution $$u_N(z,t)=N^{-4-a}(1-e^{-N^4t})\\cos(Nz),\\qquad\n f_N(z)=N^{-a}\\cos(Nz),\\qquad T_N=N^{-4},$$ of $u_t+\\partial_z^4u=f_N$ has $[\\partial_z^4u_N]_{\\alpha}$ of order $N^{\\alpha-a}$ on $[0,T_N]$. This need not tend to zero when $a=\\alpha$, whereas $a=\\alpha'$ gives precisely the power in <a href=\"#ana:fourth-small\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:fourth-small\">[ana:fourth-small]</a>. Moreover $u_t(0)=f_N$. Thus the argument gives smallness of the full spatial norm $X'$, while making no smallness assertion for the time derivative.\n\n</div>",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-flat-inverse",
        "exact_quote": "There is a number $\\delta\\in(0,1)$ such that, for every fixed $\\alpha\\in(0,1)$, the operator <a href=\"#flat:operator\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"flat:operator\">[flat:operator]</a> has a bounded real linear right inverse $$\\widehat D:Y\\longrightarrow X,\\qquad\n \\widehat{\\mathcal P}\\widehat D f=f,\\qquad\n \\|\\widehat D f\\|_X\\le C\\|f\\|_Y,\n \\qquad p_0=-2+2\\delta.$$ Here $Y$ has weight $p_0-4$ and parabolic exponent $\\alpha$, and $X$ has weight $p_0$ and includes the parabolic $\\alpha$ norms of all spatial derivatives through order four and of the first time derivative. The solution is radial and classical on $t<0$. Its initial values are selected by the construction. The constant depends only on the fixed profile, $\\delta$, $\\alpha$, and the fixed choices in the local norms.",
        "source_id": "PM-352.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "flat:recursion-solution",
          "exact_quote": "Let the one-step source increments be $$l_j=\\int_0^1S(1-s)f^\\#(j+s)\\,\\mathrm{d}s,\\qquad j\\in\\mathbb{Z}.$$ Then $\\|l_j\\|_E\\le CMq^j$. Define $$\\label{flat:recursion-solution}\n v_j=\\sum_{k=0}^\\infty P_s^k\\Pi_s l_{j-1-k}\n       -\\sum_{k=0}^\\infty P_u^{-k-1}\\Pi_u l_{j+k}.$$ Both series converge absolutely for every integer $j$. More explicitly, their norms are at most $$CMq^j\\left[\n   q^{-1}\\sum_{k=0}^\\infty(r_s/q)^k\n       +r_u^{-1}\\sum_{k=0}^\\infty(q/r_u)^k\\right]\n \\le C'Mq^j.$$ Shifting the summation indices proves $v_{j+1}=Pv_j+l_j$. On $[j,j+1]$ define $v$ by <a href=\"#flat:duhamel\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"flat:duhamel\">[flat:duhamel]</a> with initial value $v_j$. The definitions agree at the endpoints and give $$\\label{flat:preliminary-bound}\n \\|v(\\tau)\\|_E\\le CM e^{-\\gamma\\tau},\\qquad\n |V(z,t)|\\le CM\\rho^{-1+\\delta}.$$ The continuous joining creates no distributional time-boundary term, so <a href=\"#flat:similarity-equation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"flat:similarity-equation\">[flat:similarity-equation]</a> holds across every joined face. Interior regularity makes it a classical equation there. The construction is linear and real on real sources. The second sum in <a href=\"#flat:recursion-solution\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"flat:recursion-solution\">[flat:recursion-solution]</a> selects the outside spectral components using future forcing; its use is permitted precisely because no initial values have been imposed.",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "flat:annular-full",
          "exact_quote": "Apply Lemma <a href=\"#ana:l2\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ana:l2\">14</a> to <a href=\"#flat:annular-equation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"flat:annular-equation\">[flat:annular-equation]</a> on nested annuli, with the preceding-time buffer from $-4$ to $-2$. It gives $$\\label{flat:annular-full}\n \\sum_{j=0}^4\\|D_y^jU\\|_{C^\\alpha(\\mathcal U'\\times(-2,0))}\n +\\|U_s\\|_{C^\\alpha(\\mathcal U'\\times(-2,0))}\n \\le CM.$$ One first applies the estimate with terminal time $b<0$. On each such truncated slab $U$ has finite supremum norm by <a href=\"#flat:preliminary-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"flat:preliminary-bound\">[flat:preliminary-bound]</a>; the coefficient, source, and $L^2$ bounds above are independent of $b$. Letting $b\\uparrow0$ then proves <a href=\"#flat:annular-full\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"flat:annular-full\">[flat:annular-full]</a>, including Hölder quotients between times arbitrarily close to zero. The estimate uses preceding times and requires no terminal value at $s=0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There is a number $\\delta\\in(0,1)$ such that, for every fixed $\\alpha\\in(0,1)$, the operator <a href=\"#flat:operator\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"flat:operator\">[flat:operator]</a> has a bounded real linear right inverse $$\\widehat D:Y\\longrightarrow X,\\qquad\n \\widehat{\\mathcal P}\\widehat D f=f,\\qquad\n \\|\\widehat D f\\|_X\\le C\\|f\\|_Y,\n \\qquad p_0=-2+2\\delta.$$ Here $Y$ has weight $p_0-4$ and parabolic exponent $\\alpha$, and $X$ has weight $p_0$ and includes the parabolic $\\alpha$ norms of all spatial derivatives through order four and of the first time derivative. The solution is radial and classical on $t<0$. Its initial values are selected by the construction. The constant depends only on the fixed profile, $\\delta$, $\\alpha$, and the fixed choices in the local norms.",
      "explanation": "A spectral splitting in similarity coordinates constructs a bounded ancient right inverse on radial weighted spaces. Its initial values are selected using the forcing, so this is not a forward solution operator for arbitrary prescribed initial data.",

      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "flat:operator",
          "exact_quote": "Fix the profile supplied by Theorem <a href=\"#prof:profile\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prof:profile\">6</a>, and put $$\\rho=\\sqrt{-t},\\qquad x=|z|^2,\\qquad\n h(y)=\\frac{G(y)-1}{y},\\qquad\n F(z,t)=\\rho^{-1}h(|z|^2/\\rho),\\qquad z\\in\\mathbb{R}^m,$$ where $m=24$ and $h(0)=G'(0)$. Throughout this section the time domain is the entire interval $(-\\infty,0)$. The flat linearization is $$\\label{flat:operator}\n \\widehat{\\mathcal P}V\n =V_t+\\frac{A(z,t)}{16}\\Delta_z^2V+B(z,t)V,\n \\quad\n A=G(|z|^2/\\rho)^2,\\quad\n B=2|z|^2G(|z|^2/\\rho)\\widehat{\\mathcal A}F,\n \\qquad \\widehat{\\mathcal A}=\\frac1{16}\\Delta_z^2.$$ The spaces below are the radial versions of Definition <a href=\"#ana:spaces\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ana:spaces\">12</a>, with scale $\\sigma=(|z|^2+\\rho)^{1/2}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-glue-solution",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be the profile of Theorem <a href=\"#prof:profile\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prof:profile\">6</a>, put $$h(y)=\\frac{G(y)-1}{y},\\qquad\n F(t,x)=\\rho^{-1}h(x/\\rho),$$ and fix $0<\\alpha<\\alpha'<1$. Choose $\\delta\\in(0,1)$ and the ancient right inverse supplied by Theorem <a href=\"#flat:inverse\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"flat:inverse\">18</a>. For every sufficiently small $\\varepsilon>0$, there are $T>0$ and a real invariant function $V\\in X_\\alpha$ on $\\Sigma\\times[-T,0)$ such that $\\lVert V\\rVert_{X'_\\alpha}\\le\\varepsilon$ and $H=F+V$ satisfies $$\\label{glue:equation}\n H_t+(1+v_*H)^2\\mathcal AH=0,\\qquad v_*=x(1-x).$$ The function $H$ is smooth on $\\Sigma\\times(-T,0)$, and there are constants $0<c_1<c_2<\\infty$, independent of $t$, such that $$\\label{glue:positive}\n c_1\\le 1+v_*H\\le c_2.$$ In particular, $H(t,\\cdot)$ is smooth as a function of $x\\in[0,1]$, and $$\\label{glue:center}\n V(t,0)=O(\\rho^{-1+\\delta})\\qquad(t\\uparrow0).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-352.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-glue-special-error-2",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-glue-special-error</summary>\n\n\n\n\n\n*Proof of Theorem <a href=\"#glue:solution\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"glue:solution\">19</a>.* Expanding <a href=\"#glue:equation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:equation\">[glue:equation]</a> at $F$ gives $$\\label{glue:nonlinear-equation}\n \\mathcal PV+E_c+N(V)=0,$$ where $$\\label{glue:nonlinearity}\n N(V)=v_*^2V^2\\mathcal AF+\n       \\bigl(2v_*g_cV+v_*^2V^2\\bigr)\\mathcal AV.$$ This expression contains only spatial derivatives of $V$, so is defined on $X'_\\alpha$. For clarity, on a box of scale $b$, a function with $\\lVert V\\rVert_{X'_\\alpha}\\le\\varepsilon$ has scaled sizes $$V=O(\\varepsilon b^{-2+2\\delta}),\\qquad\n \\mathcal AV=O(\\varepsilon b^{-6+2\\delta}),$$ where these statements include the scaled parabolic $C^\\alpha$ norms. By Lemma <a href=\"#glue:background\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"glue:background\">20</a>, the two quadratic terms in <a href=\"#glue:nonlinearity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:nonlinearity\">[glue:nonlinearity]</a> therefore have size $C\\varepsilon^2b^{-6+4\\delta}$, and the cubic term has size $C\\varepsilon^3b^{-6+6\\delta}$. Relative to the $Y$ weight these gain $b^{2\\delta}$ and $b^{4\\delta}$, respectively. Since $b$ is bounded above on the compact domain, Hölder multiplication and the factorization of differences of products give uniform bounds $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\lVert N(V)\\rVert_{Y_\\alpha}&\\le C_N(\\varepsilon^2+\\varepsilon^3),\n \\label{glue:nonlinear-size}\\\\\n \\lVert N(V)-N(W)\\rVert_{Y_\\alpha}\n &\\le C_N(\\varepsilon+\\varepsilon^2)\\lVert V-W\\rVert_{X'_\\alpha}\n \\label{glue:nonlinear-lipschitz}\n\\end{aligned}$$ on the closed ball of radius $\\varepsilon$ in $X'_\\alpha$. The fixed sphere coefficients in $\\mathcal A$ have bounded rescaled norms, so these estimates apply on the whole sphere, including the other endpoint $x=1$.\n\nWe specify the parameter choices to avoid a dependence on an inverse norm that is still being chosen. First fix the profile, exponents, flat inverse, and the constants $\\beta_0,\\eta_0,C_D$ of Proposition <a href=\"#glue:inverse\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"glue:inverse\">22</a>. The nonlinear constant $C_N$ is uniform throughout this parameter range. Choose the requested ball radius $\\varepsilon>0$ so small that $$C_D C_N(\\varepsilon+\\varepsilon^2)\\le\\tfrac14,\\qquad\n C_*\\varepsilon\\le\\tfrac12\\mu,$$ where $C_*$ bounds $\\lvert v_*V\\rvert/\\lVert V\\rVert_{X'_\\alpha}$ uniformly. Such a $C_*$ exists because $v_*V$ has scaled size $C\\varepsilon b^{2\\delta}$ and $b\\le\\sqrt2$. Now take $\\beta\\le\\beta_0$ sufficiently small, and next $\\eta\\le\\min(\\eta_0,a_0^4)$ sufficiently small, that Lemma <a href=\"#glue:special-error\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"glue:special-error\">23</a> gives $\\lVert DE_c\\rVert_{X'_\\alpha}\\le\\varepsilon/2$; put $T=\\eta\\beta^4$.\n\nThe map $$\\mathcal T(V)=-DE_c-DN(V)$$ takes the closed $X'_\\alpha$ ball of radius $\\varepsilon$ into itself by <a href=\"#glue:nonlinear-size\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:nonlinear-size\">[glue:nonlinear-size]</a>, and is a contraction by <a href=\"#glue:nonlinear-lipschitz\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:nonlinear-lipschitz\">[glue:nonlinear-lipschitz]</a>. The real invariant subspace is closed, so the Banach fixed point theorem gives a real invariant fixed point there. Its fixed point identity also shows that this same $V$ belongs to $X_\\alpha$, because $E_c+N(V)\\in Y_\\alpha$ and $D:Y_\\alpha\\to X_\\alpha$. Applying $\\mathcal PD=I$ therefore yields <a href=\"#glue:nonlinear-equation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:nonlinear-equation\">[glue:nonlinear-equation]</a> in the classical Hölder sense and proves <a href=\"#glue:equation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:equation\">[glue:equation]</a>. This recovers the time derivative after the contraction has been performed.\n\nBy <a href=\"#glue:background-positive\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:background-positive\">[glue:background-positive]</a> and the radius choice, $$\\tfrac12\\mu\\le 1+v_*(F+V)\\le M+\\tfrac12\\mu,$$ which proves <a href=\"#glue:positive\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:positive\">[glue:positive]</a>. On each compact time subinterval of $(-T,0)$, the weight is bounded above and below, and the equation for $H$ is a uniformly parabolic scalar equation with principal multiplier $(1+v_*H)^2$. Its current regularity is $C^{4+\\alpha,1+\\alpha/4}$. Interior spatial difference quotients in this equation have the same principal operator; their sources are bounded in $C^\\alpha$ because the coefficient is a smooth function of $x,H$ and $H$ already has four spatial derivatives. The interior estimate of Lemma <a href=\"#ana:schauder\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ana:schauder\">13</a>, followed by passage to the difference-quotient limit on a smaller cylinder, gains one spatial derivative. Repetition gains every spatial derivative. The equation then gives corresponding spatial bounds for $H_t$; differentiating the equation in time and repeating the interior estimates gives all mixed derivatives. Thus $H$ is smooth on $\\Sigma\\times(-T,0)$. Invariant smoothness gives smoothness in $x\\in[0,1]$ by Lemma <a href=\"#geo:operators\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"geo:operators\">4</a>. At $x=0$, the $X'$ weight reads $$\\lvert V(t,0)\\rvert\\le\\varepsilon\\sigma(t,0)^{-2+2\\delta}\n                =\\varepsilon\\rho^{-1+\\delta},$$ up to the fixed equivalent-norm constant. This proves <a href=\"#glue:center\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:center\">[glue:center]</a> and finishes the construction. ◻",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $G$ be the profile of Theorem <a href=\"#prof:profile\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prof:profile\">6</a>, put $$h(y)=\\frac{G(y)-1}{y},\\qquad\n F(t,x)=\\rho^{-1}h(x/\\rho),$$ and fix $0<\\alpha<\\alpha'<1$. Choose $\\delta\\in(0,1)$ and the ancient right inverse supplied by Theorem <a href=\"#flat:inverse\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"flat:inverse\">18</a>. For every sufficiently small $\\varepsilon>0$, there are $T>0$ and a real invariant function $V\\in X_\\alpha$ on $\\Sigma\\times[-T,0)$ such that $\\lVert V\\rVert_{X'_\\alpha}\\le\\varepsilon$ and $H=F+V$ satisfies $$\\label{glue:equation}\n H_t+(1+v_*H)^2\\mathcal AH=0,\\qquad v_*=x(1-x).$$ The function $H$ is smooth on $\\Sigma\\times(-T,0)$, and there are constants $0<c_1<c_2<\\infty$, independent of $t$, such that $$\\label{glue:positive}\n c_1\\le 1+v_*H\\le c_2.$$ In particular, $H(t,\\cdot)$ is smooth as a function of $x\\in[0,1]$, and $$\\label{glue:center}\n V(t,0)=O(\\rho^{-1+\\delta})\\qquad(t\\uparrow0).$$",
      "explanation": "The flat shrinker admits a small invariant correction on the compact auxiliary sphere. The corrected solution stays positive, is smooth before the singular time, and preserves the lower-order origin correction required for curvature divergence.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-glue-solution",
          "exact_quote": "and fix $0<\\alpha<\\alpha'<1$.",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-glue-solution",
          "exact_quote": "Choose $\\delta\\in(0,1)$ and the ancient right inverse supplied by Theorem",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prof-profile",
          "exact_quote": "There exist a constant $c>0$ and a function $G\\in C^\\infty([0,\\infty))$ such that $$\\label{prof:profile-equation}\n Q(y\\partial_y)(G-1)=\\frac{y^2}{2}\\left(\\frac1G-\\frac1c\\right),\n \\qquad Q(D)=(D-1)(D+9)(D+10).$$ The function $G$ is bounded above and bounded away from zero, satisfies $G(0)=1$ and $G'(0)<0$, and is analytic near zero. There are real coefficients $g_j$, $j\\ge1$, such that, for every $J,\\ell\\ge0$, $$\\label{prof:expansion}\n \\frac{\\mathrm{d}^\\ell}{\\mathrm{d}y^\\ell}\n \\left(G(y)-c-\\sum_{j=1}^{J}g_jy^{-2j}\\right)\n =O\\bigl(y^{-2(J+1)-\\ell}\\bigr)\\qquad(y\\longrightarrow\\infty).$$ In particular, $u\\mapsto G(u^{-1/2})$ extends smoothly to $u=0$ from the right, with value $c$.*\n\n\n*Set $h(y)=(G(y)-1)/y$, with its smooth value at zero. For $t<0$, the function $$\\label{prof:flat-shrinker}\n F(t,x)=\\rho^{-1}h(x/\\rho),\\qquad \\rho=\\sqrt{-t},$$ solves $$F_t+(1+xF)^2\\widehat{\\mathcal A}F=0,\n \\qquad \\widehat{\\mathcal A}=(x\\partial_x^2+12\\partial_x)^2.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-flat-inverse",
          "exact_quote": "There is a number $\\delta\\in(0,1)$ such that, for every fixed $\\alpha\\in(0,1)$, the operator <a href=\"#flat:operator\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"flat:operator\">[flat:operator]</a> has a bounded real linear right inverse $$\\widehat D:Y\\longrightarrow X,\\qquad\n \\widehat{\\mathcal P}\\widehat D f=f,\\qquad\n \\|\\widehat D f\\|_X\\le C\\|f\\|_Y,\n \\qquad p_0=-2+2\\delta.$$ Here $Y$ has weight $p_0-4$ and parabolic exponent $\\alpha$, and $X$ has weight $p_0$ and includes the parabolic $\\alpha$ norms of all spatial derivatives through order four and of the first time derivative. The solution is radial and classical on $t<0$. Its initial values are selected by the construction. The constant depends only on the fixed profile, $\\delta$, $\\alpha$, and the fixed choices in the local norms.",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }
      ],

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-glue-background",
        "exact_quote": "For $0<T\\le1$, on the compact domain one has $$\\label{glue:background-bounds}\n F=O_{\\mathrm{sc}}(b^{-2}),\\qquad\n \\mathcal AF=O_{\\mathrm{sc}}(b^{-6}),\\qquad\n A_c=O_{\\mathrm{sc}}(1),\\qquad\n B_c=O_{\\mathrm{sc}}(b^{-4}),\\qquad\n v_*=O_{\\mathrm{sc}}(b^2).$$ The analogous flat bounds hold in a fixed neighborhood of $r=0$. In the common coordinates there, write $$\\mathcal P-\\widehat\\mathcal P=\\sum_{|\\nu|\\le4}d_\\nu(z,t)\\partial_z^\\nu.$$ For each $j,k\\ge0$, the rescaled coefficients satisfy $$\\label{glue:mismatch-coeff}\n b^{4-|\\nu|+j+4k}\n \\lvert \\partial_z^j\\partial_t^k d_\\nu\\rvert\\le C_{j,k}b^2.$$ Finally, near $r=0$, $$\\label{glue:error-order}\n E_c=O_{\\mathrm{sc}}(b^{-4}).$$ Away from a fixed neighborhood of $r=0$, all these background quantities extend smoothly to $t=0$ with bounds of every finite order.",
        "source_id": "PM-352.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-glue-background",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-glue-background</summary>\n\n\n*Proof.* In the region $r^2\\le C\\rho$, the formula for $F$, smoothness of $h$ at zero, and the change $z=\\sqrt\\rho\\,\\zeta$ give $$\\lvert \\partial_z^j\\partial_t^k F\\rvert\n \\le C_{j,k}\\rho^{-1-j/2-2k}\n \\le C'_{j,k}\\sigma^{-2-j-4k}.$$ In the region $r^2\\ge\\rho$, use the differentiable expansion from Theorem <a href=\"#prof:profile\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prof:profile\">6</a>. For arbitrary $N$ it has the form $$\\label{glue:outer-expansion}\n F(t,r^2)=\\frac{c-1}{r^2}\n       +\\sum_{\\ell=1}^{N}a_\\ell\\frac{(-t)^\\ell}{r^{4\\ell+2}}\n       +\\text{remainder}.$$ The all-order remainder estimates allow $N$ to be chosen larger than any prescribed number of differentiations. Differentiating the displayed terms and the remainder gives $\\lvert \\partial_z^j\\partial_t^kF\\rvert\\le C_{j,k}r^{-2-j-4k}$. This proves the first bound and also proves the asserted smooth extension away from the core. In particular it supplies time derivative bounds up to the open terminal face, which do not follow from spatial decay alone.\n\nNear the core, write a point of the sphere as $(z,\\sqrt{1-|z|^2}\\,\\theta)$, $\\theta\\in S^5$. Invariant functions are independent of $\\theta$, and on these functions $$\\label{glue:laplace-coordinate}\n \\Delta_\\Sigma\n =\\Delta_z-\\sum_{i,j}z_i z_j\\partial_{ij}\n             -29\\sum_i z_i\\partial_i.$$ The product coordinates and ordinary sphere coordinates have uniformly controlled transition maps on a fixed smaller neighborhood. Thus their scaled norms are uniformly equivalent for the functions considered here. Lemma <a href=\"#geo:operators\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"geo:operators\">4</a> gives $$\\mathcal A=\\frac1{16}\\Delta_\\Sigma^2\n       -\\frac{37}{4}\\Delta_\\Sigma+312,\n \\qquad \\widehat\\mathcal A=\\frac1{16}\\Delta_z^2.$$ In $\\mathcal A-\\widehat\\mathcal A$, the coefficients of orders four, three, and two have sizes $O(r^2)$, $O(r)$, and $O(1)$, respectively; those of orders one and zero are smooth and bounded. After multiplication by $b^4$ and rescaling space by $b$, every coefficient is $O_{\\mathrm{sc}}(b^2)$. For example the first three sizes become $O(r^2)$, $O(br)$, and $O(b^2)$, and $r\\le b$. Differentiating these polynomial coefficients preserves the stated scaled bound. The bounds for $\\mathcal AF$ follow; away from this coordinate neighborhood they follow from the smooth extension and the positive lower bound for $\\sigma$.\n\nThe function $v_*=x-x^2$ has size $O_{\\mathrm{sc}}(b^2)$, and $v_*-x=-x^2=O_{\\mathrm{sc}}(b^4)$. Hence $$g_c-g_f=-x^2F=O_{\\mathrm{sc}}(b^2),\\qquad\n A_c-A_f=O_{\\mathrm{sc}}(b^2).$$ Products give the remaining bounds in <a href=\"#glue:background-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:background-bounds\">[glue:background-bounds]</a>. For the zeroth-order mismatch, use $$B_c-B_f\n =2(v_*g_c-xg_f)\\widehat\\mathcal AF\n        +2v_*g_c(\\mathcal A-\\widehat\\mathcal A)F.$$ The two terms have size $O_{\\mathrm{sc}}(b^{-2})$, since $v_*g_c-xg_f=O_{\\mathrm{sc}}(b^4)$ and $(\\mathcal A-\\widehat\\mathcal A)F=O_{\\mathrm{sc}}(b^{-4})$. Together with the principal multiplier difference, this proves <a href=\"#glue:mismatch-coeff\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:mismatch-coeff\">[glue:mismatch-coeff]</a> in every derivative order. Finally, $$E_c=(A_c-A_f)\\widehat\\mathcal AF+A_c(\\mathcal A-\\widehat\\mathcal A)F\n      =O_{\\mathrm{sc}}(b^{-4}),$$ using the exact flat equation, as required. ◻",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For $0<T\\le1$, on the compact domain one has $$\\label{glue:background-bounds}\n F=O_{\\mathrm{sc}}(b^{-2}),\\qquad\n \\mathcal AF=O_{\\mathrm{sc}}(b^{-6}),\\qquad\n A_c=O_{\\mathrm{sc}}(1),\\qquad\n B_c=O_{\\mathrm{sc}}(b^{-4}),\\qquad\n v_*=O_{\\mathrm{sc}}(b^2).$$ The analogous flat bounds hold in a fixed neighborhood of $r=0$. In the common coordinates there, write $$\\mathcal P-\\widehat\\mathcal P=\\sum_{|\\nu|\\le4}d_\\nu(z,t)\\partial_z^\\nu.$$ For each $j,k\\ge0$, the rescaled coefficients satisfy $$\\label{glue:mismatch-coeff}\n b^{4-|\\nu|+j+4k}\n \\lvert \\partial_z^j\\partial_t^k d_\\nu\\rvert\\le C_{j,k}b^2.$$ Finally, near $r=0$, $$\\label{glue:error-order}\n E_c=O_{\\mathrm{sc}}(b^{-4}).$$ Away from a fixed neighborhood of $r=0$, all these background quantities extend smoothly to $t=0$ with bounds of every finite order.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-glue-extension",
        "exact_quote": "There is a real linear extension $\\mathcal E_i:Y_\\alpha\\to\nY_\\alpha(\\mathbb{R}^m\\times(-\\infty,0))$, uniformly bounded for $T\\le\\beta^4$, which equals $\\chi_i f$ in flat coordinates for $-T\\le t<0$, and is supported spatially in $r\\le2\\beta$.",
        "source_id": "PM-352.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-glue-extension",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-glue-extension</summary>\n\n*Proof.* Put $f_i=\\chi_i f$, view it in the flat coordinates, and extend it by zero beyond their neighborhood. The spatial extension is bounded because the cutoff vanishes before the boundary of that neighborhood. The $Y_\\alpha$ norm gives a spatial $C^\\alpha$ trace $f_i(-T,z)$ at the lower time face. Choose a smooth $\\psi:[0,\\infty)\\to[0,1]$ equal to one near zero and zero for arguments at least one. For $t<-T$ set $$\\label{glue:extension-formula}\n (\\mathcal E_i f)(t,z)\n =f_i(-T,z)\\,\\psi\\left(\\frac{-t-T}{(|z|^2+\\sqrt T)^2}\\right).$$ Let $b_0=(|z|^2+\\sqrt T)^{1/2}$. Wherever this expression is nonzero, $$T\\le-t\\le T+b_0^4,\n \\qquad b_0^2\\le |z|^2+\\sqrt{-t}\\le(1+\\sqrt2)b_0^2.$$ The ancient scale is therefore comparable to $b_0$. The argument of $\\psi$ has bounded derivatives in spatial units $b_0$ and temporal units $b_0^4$ on its transition region. The trace norm, Hölder multiplication, and scale comparability prove the required estimate below $-T$. Across that face the values agree. Splitting a temporal increment at $-T$ bounds its $\\alpha/4$ Hölder quotient by the sum of the two one-sided bounds; a spatial increment is handled by the trace bound on comparable boxes. This proves the full parabolic $C^\\alpha$ bound. No time derivative of the source is required. The construction is real, linear, and radial. Its continuation below $-T$ is in fact supported in $-26\\beta^4\\le t<-T$, though a temporal support restriction is unnecessary for the ancient inverse. ◻",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There is a real linear extension $\\mathcal E_i:Y_\\alpha\\to\nY_\\alpha(\\mathbb{R}^m\\times(-\\infty,0))$, uniformly bounded for $T\\le\\beta^4$, which equals $\\chi_i f$ in flat coordinates for $-T\\le t<0$, and is supported spatially in $r\\le2\\beta$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-glue-inverse",
        "exact_quote": "There are $\\beta_0,\\eta_0,C_D>0$ such that, for $0<\\beta\\le\\beta_0$ and $0<T\\le\\eta_0\\beta^4$, the operator $\\mathcal P$ has a real invariant right inverse $D:Y_\\alpha\\to X_\\alpha$ with $\\lVert D\\rVert\\le C_D$. No initial value is prescribed for $Df$.",
        "source_id": "PM-352.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-glue-inverse",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-glue-inverse</summary>\n\n\n*Proof.* Define $$D_{\\mathrm{app}}f=V_i+V_o,\\qquad \\mathcal R=R_i+J_o.$$ Equations <a href=\"#glue:inner-identity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:inner-identity\">[glue:inner-identity]</a>, <a href=\"#glue:outer-source\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:outer-source\">[glue:outer-source]</a>, and <a href=\"#glue:outer-identity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:outer-identity\">[glue:outer-identity]</a> give the exact operator identity $$\\label{glue:parametrix}\n \\mathcal PD_{\\mathrm{app}}=I+\\mathcal R,\\qquad\n \\lVert D_{\\mathrm{app}}\\rVert_{Y_\\alpha\\to X_\\alpha}\\le C,\\qquad\n \\lVert \\mathcal R\\rVert_{Y_\\alpha\\to Y_\\alpha}\n \\le C\\bigl(\\beta^2+\\eta^{(1-\\alpha)/4}\\bigr).$$ Choose $\\beta_0$ and then $\\eta_0$ small enough that the last bound is at most $1/2$ throughout the asserted range, decreasing the earlier $\\eta_0$ if necessary. The convergent Neumann series defines $$\\label{glue:neumann}\n D=D_{\\mathrm{app}}(I+\\mathcal R)^{-1}.$$ It satisfies $\\mathcal PD=I$, is real and invariant, and has uniform norm at most twice the bound for $D_{\\mathrm{app}}$. This is a right inverse construction and requires no assertion of injectivity. ◻",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There are $\\beta_0,\\eta_0,C_D>0$ such that, for $0<\\beta\\le\\beta_0$ and $0<T\\le\\eta_0\\beta^4$, the operator $\\mathcal P$ has a real invariant right inverse $D:Y_\\alpha\\to X_\\alpha$ with $\\lVert D\\rVert\\le C_D$. No initial value is prescribed for $Df$.",
      "explanation": "The localized ancient inverse and outer zero-data solution combine into a compact parametrix. Exact cancellation of the inner cutoff commutator leaves small errors that a Neumann series removes.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "glue:DE-small",
        "exact_quote": "For the right inverses of Proposition <a href=\"#glue:inverse\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"glue:inverse\">22</a>, $$\\label{glue:DE-small}\n \\lVert DE_c\\rVert_{X'_\\alpha}\n \\le C\\left(\\beta^{2-2\\delta}\n       +\\eta^{(\\alpha'-\\alpha)/4}\n       +\\eta^{(1-\\alpha)/4}\\right).$$ In particular this norm becomes arbitrarily small by choosing $\\beta$ and then $\\eta=T/\\beta^4$ sufficiently small.",
        "source_id": "PM-352.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-glue-special-error",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-glue-special-error</summary>\n\n*Proof.* Use the inner output cutoff, rather than the inner input cutoff, to split $$E_c=f_c+f_e,\\qquad f_c=\\chi_bE_c,\\qquad f_e=(1-\\chi_b)E_c.$$ Equation <a href=\"#glue:core-error-small\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:core-error-small\">[glue:core-error-small]</a> gives $\\lVert Df_c\\rVert_{X_\\alpha}\\le C\\beta^{2-2\\delta}$. The remaining source vanishes for $r\\le2\\beta$, so $\\chi_i f_e=0$ exactly. Its inner extension, flat correction, $R_i f_e$, and $J_i f_e$ all vanish. Thus $D_{\\mathrm{app}}f_e$ consists solely of the outer zero-data solution for source $f_e$, followed by multiplication by $\\chi_o$, and $\\mathcal R f_e=J_o f_e$.\n\n\nThe extra regularity in Lemma <a href=\"#glue:background\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"glue:background\">20</a> gives $$\\label{glue:outer-error-high}\n \\lVert f_e\\rVert_{Y_{\\alpha'}(s_\\beta)}\\le C.$$ The constant is uniform in $\\beta,T$: in the transition annulus all cutoff derivatives have bounded scaled size, and elsewhere this is <a href=\"#glue:error-weight\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:error-weight\">[glue:error-weight]</a> or the fixed exterior smooth bound. Apply Lemma <a href=\"#ana:short\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ana:short\">15</a> at exponent $\\alpha'$ to the outer zero-data problem with source $f_e$. The coefficient bounds hold at that exponent, so its solution has uniformly bounded $X_{\\alpha'}(s_\\beta)$ norm. Equation <a href=\"#ana:fourth-small\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:fourth-small\">[ana:fourth-small]</a> of Lemma <a href=\"#ana:interpolation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ana:interpolation\">16</a> gives an $X'_\\alpha(s_\\beta)$ bound of $C\\eta^{(\\alpha'-\\alpha)/4}$. Multiplication by $\\chi_o$ is bounded in these scaled spaces, and $s_\\beta$ is comparable to $\\sigma$ on the output support. This proves the first estimate below; the commutator bound <a href=\"#glue:outer-error-small\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:outer-error-small\">[glue:outer-error-small]</a> proves the second: $$\\label{glue:app-special-small}\n \\lVert D_{\\mathrm{app}}f_e\\rVert_{X'_\\alpha}\n \\le C\\eta^{(\\alpha'-\\alpha)/4},\\qquad\n \\lVert \\mathcal Rf_e\\rVert_{Y_\\alpha}\n \\le C\\eta^{(1-\\alpha)/4}.$$ The first estimate includes the temporal Hölder seminorms of all spatial derivatives through order four, as supplied by <a href=\"#ana:fourth-small\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:fourth-small\">[ana:fourth-small]</a>. It makes no claim that the first time derivative is small: the outer zero-data solution has time derivative $f_e$ at the initial face.\n\nFinally, <a href=\"#glue:neumann\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:neumann\">[glue:neumann]</a> gives $D(I+\\mathcal R)=D_{\\mathrm{app}}$, so $$\\label{glue:special-neumann-identity}\n Df_e=D_{\\mathrm{app}}f_e-D\\mathcal Rf_e.$$ The uniform bound for $D$ and <a href=\"#glue:app-special-small\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:app-special-small\">[glue:app-special-small]</a> prove the desired estimate for $f_e$. Combining it with the estimate for $f_c$ proves <a href=\"#glue:DE-small\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:DE-small\">[glue:DE-small]</a>. ◻",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For the right inverses of Proposition <a href=\"#glue:inverse\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"glue:inverse\">22</a>, $$\\label{glue:DE-small}\n \\lVert DE_c\\rVert_{X'_\\alpha}\n \\le C\\left(\\beta^{2-2\\delta}\n       +\\eta^{(\\alpha'-\\alpha)/4}\n       +\\eta^{(1-\\alpha)/4}\\right).$$ In particular this norm becomes arbitrarily small by choosing $\\beta$ and then $\\eta=T/\\beta^4$ sufficiently small.",
      "explanation": "Although the compact inverse need not have small operator norm, its value on the specific background residual is small in the spatial parabolic norm. Core localization and a higher source Hölder exponent supply the separate small factors.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cert-shoot",
        "exact_quote": "For every parameter $z_*\\in[-R,R]^2$, where $R=5\\cdot10^{-10}$, let $(k,d)$ be given by <a href=\"#prof:parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:parameters\">[prof:parameters]</a>, and let $p$ be the origin solution of <a href=\"#prof:ode\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:ode\">[prof:ode]</a> supplied by Lemma <a href=\"#prof:origin\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prof:origin\">7</a>. This solution extends to $0\\le s\\le S_0=56$ and satisfies $$1+p(s)>0.$$ For the coordinates of its jet relative to $p_{\\rm a}$, defined in <a href=\"#prof:coordinates\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:coordinates\">[prof:coordinates]</a>, one has $$\\label{cert:conclusion}\n |q|\\le .25R,\\qquad\n \\big\\|(\\Re\\xi,\\Im\\xi)-z_*\\big\\|_\\infty\\le .13R.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-352.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "cert:nonlinear",
          "exact_quote": "#### Uniform nonlinear error and closure of the bootstrap\n\n\nFix $z_*\\in[-R,R]^2$. On each block compare its actual shot with the reference solution on that block, and write $\\delta p=p-p_{\\rm ref}$. For the only nonlinear term in the physical third-component equation, $$-\\frac{p}{1+p}\n +\\frac{p_{\\rm ref}}{1+p_{\\rm ref}}\n +\\frac{\\delta p}{(1+p_{\\rm ref})^2}\n =\\frac{(\\delta p)^2}{(1+p_{\\rm ref})^2(1+p)}.$$ It follows that the difference equation, beyond the reference linearization, has forcing $e_3\\mathcal N$, where $$\\label{cert:nonlinear-remainder}\n |\\mathcal N|=\n \\frac{|\\delta p|^2}{|1+p_{\\rm ref}|^2|1+p|}.$$ The $d$-dependence is linear and is already included in the fourth variation component.\n\nTemporarily assume $|\\delta p|\\le14R$ throughout the portions of the blocks reached so far. Since $m_j>.23$ and $m_j/(m_j-14R)<1.001$, <a href=\"#cert:nonlinear-remainder\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cert:nonlinear-remainder\">[cert:nonlinear-remainder]</a> implies $$|\\mathcal N|\\le\\frac{1.001(14R)^2}{m_j^3}.$$ The error accumulated by the end of block $j$, before multiplication by subsequent complete-block transfers, has zero fourth component. Its norm is bounded by $$10^{-19}\n  +4.3\\,\\ell_j\\frac{1.001(14R)^2}{m_j^3}.$$ The first term is the reference jump <a href=\"#cert:local-errors\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cert:local-errors\">[cert:local-errors]</a>; the second follows by variation of constants and the third-column estimate in <a href=\"#cert:partial-transfer\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cert:partial-transfer\">[cert:partial-transfer]</a>.\n\n\nDefine the endpoint error $e_i$ by $$\\begin{pmatrix}p(s_i)\\\\p'(s_i)\\\\p''(s_i)\\\\d\\end{pmatrix}\n =\\begin{pmatrix}U_i\\\\d_0\\end{pmatrix}\n          +T_{i,0}Zz_*+e_i.$$ Its fourth component is zero. By <a href=\"#cert:initial-error\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cert:initial-error\">[cert:initial-error]</a>, <a href=\"#cert:exact-sums\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cert:exact-sums\">[cert:exact-sums]</a>, and the preceding local error estimate, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|e_i\\|_\\infty\n &\\le580000\\cdot10^{-19}+8501\\cdot(2\\cdot10^{-19})\n       +4.3\\cdot250000\\cdot1.001(14R)^2\\notag\\\\\n &=.1055747504R<.11R.\n \\label{cert:endpoint-error}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For an interior point of a block, the first-row estimate in <a href=\"#cert:partial-transfer\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cert:partial-transfer\">[cert:partial-transfer]</a>, <a href=\"#cert:linear-size\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cert:linear-size\">[cert:linear-size]</a>, and the forcing estimate give $$\\begin{aligned}\n |\\delta p|\n &\\le4.3(2.72+.11)R\n      +4.3\\frac{1.001(14R)^2}{.23^3}\\notag\\\\\n &<12.169035R<14R.\n \\label{cert:bootstrap-improvement}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here replacing the remaining block length by $1$ is a harmless overestimate. This improves the bootstrap strictly both at block starts and in block interiors, including the reference jumps. At $s=1$ it starts from $\\|Z\\|<.02$ and <a href=\"#cert:initial-error\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cert:initial-error\">[cert:initial-error]</a>. Continuation over the finitely many blocks therefore proves <a href=\"#cert:endpoint-error\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cert:endpoint-error\">[cert:endpoint-error]</a> at every block endpoint.\n\nFor clarity, existence is part of this continuation argument. As long as the bootstrap holds, $1+p\\ge .23-14R>0$. On the finite interval $1\\le s\\le56$, the equations for $p'$ and $p''$ form a linear system with bounded coefficients and bounded forcing once $p$ stays in this range. Gronwall’s inequality bounds these derivatives on every such interval. Thus no other finite-time escape can interrupt continuation. Positivity before $s=1$ was established in Subsection <a href=\"#cert:origin-error\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cert:origin-error\">7.2</a>. This proves the existence and positivity statements of Proposition <a href=\"#cert:shoot\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cert:shoot\">24</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "cert:projection",
          "exact_quote": "#### Projection at the matching point\n\nLet $\\Gamma$ denote the exact real coordinate matrix of <a href=\"#prof:coordinates\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:coordinates\">[prof:coordinates]</a>. Lemma <a href=\"#prof:root-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prof:root-bounds\">8</a> gives $$|\\Gamma_{ab}-\\Lambda_{ab}|<.001,\n \\qquad\n \\|\\Gamma_{1,\\cdot}\\|_{\\rm row}<.92,\\qquad\n \\|\\Gamma_{a,\\cdot}\\|_{\\rm row}<.68\\quad(a=2,3).$$ Thus $\\|\\Gamma-\\Lambda\\|<.003$. The polynomial $P_{\\rm a}$ also satisfies $$\\label{cert:pa-variation}\n \\|P_{\\rm a}(d)-P_{\\rm a}(d_0)\\|_\\infty\n \\le .01|d-d_0|\\le .01\\cdot.011R;$$ this follows by differentiating the four explicit coefficients in <a href=\"#prof:asymptotic-polynomial\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:asymptotic-polynomial\">[prof:asymptotic-polynomial]</a>, as detailed in the proof of Lemma <a href=\"#prof:tail-residual\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prof:tail-residual\">9</a>.\n\nSet $A_*=U_N-P_{\\rm a}(d_0)$. The endpoint error and transfer error give $$\\begin{pmatrix}p\\\\p'\\\\p''\\end{pmatrix}(56)-P_{\\rm a}(d)\n = A_*+Yz_*+\\epsilon_*\n       -\\bigl(P_{\\rm a}(d)-P_{\\rm a}(d_0)\\bigr),\n \\qquad\n \\|\\epsilon_*\\|_\\infty\\le\n       \\bigl(.11+.001\\cdot.02\\bigr)R.$$ In fact $\\epsilon_*$ is the sum of the top three entries of $e_N$ and $((T_{N,0}-\\widetilde T_{N,0})Zz_*)_{\\mathrm{top}\\,3}$. Apply one row of $\\Gamma$. The contribution of $A_*$, using <a href=\"#cert:finite-matching\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cert:finite-matching\">[cert:finite-matching]</a>, is at most $(.006+.003\\cdot.04)R$. For the first row the linear contribution is bounded by $(.116+.003\\cdot2.7)R$. For row $a=2,3$, after subtracting the corresponding coordinate of $z_*$, it is bounded by $(.001+.003\\cdot2.7)R$. The remaining contribution is bounded by the row norm of $\\Gamma$ times $$\\bigl(.11+.001\\cdot.02+.01\\cdot.011\\bigr)R.$$ Consequently, in units of $R$, the two required estimates are $$\\begin{aligned}\n |q|/R\n &\\le .006+.003\\cdot.04+.116+.003\\cdot2.7\n       +.92(.11+.001\\cdot.02+.01\\cdot.011)\\\\\n &=.2315396<.25,\\\\\n \\big\\|(\\Re\\xi,\\Im\\xi)-z_*\\big\\|_\\infty/R\n &\\le .006+.003\\cdot.04+.001+.003\\cdot2.7\n       +.68(.11+.001\\cdot.02+.01\\cdot.011)\\\\\n &=.0901084<.13.\n\\end{aligned}$$ These are exact rational comparisons and establish <a href=\"#cert:conclusion\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cert:conclusion\">[cert:conclusion]</a>, completing the proof of Proposition <a href=\"#cert:shoot\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cert:shoot\">24</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every parameter $z_*\\in[-R,R]^2$, where $R=5\\cdot10^{-10}$, let $(k,d)$ be given by <a href=\"#prof:parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:parameters\">[prof:parameters]</a>, and let $p$ be the origin solution of <a href=\"#prof:ode\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:ode\">[prof:ode]</a> supplied by Lemma <a href=\"#prof:origin\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prof:origin\">7</a>. This solution extends to $0\\le s\\le S_0=56$ and satisfies $$1+p(s)>0.$$ For the coordinates of its jet relative to $p_{\\rm a}$, defined in <a href=\"#prof:coordinates\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:coordinates\">[prof:coordinates]</a>, one has $$\\label{cert:conclusion}\n |q|\\le .25R,\\qquad\n \\big\\|(\\Re\\xi,\\Im\\xi)-z_*\\big\\|_\\infty\\le .13R.$$",
      "explanation": "Validated finite integration and nonlinear parameter estimates control the entire shooting square. Every shot reaches the matching point with positivity and with stable and growing coordinates within the tolerances needed for matching.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cert-shoot",
          "exact_quote": "For every parameter $z_*\\in[-R,R]^2$, where $R=5\\cdot10^{-10}$,",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "prof:parameters",
          "exact_quote": "The affine square below makes the shot’s two remaining coordinates close to its parameter $z_*$. Together with the tail bound, the certified estimate yields a continuous self-map of the square; Brouwer’s theorem supplies a matching point. All terminating decimals in this section denote exact rational numbers. Define $$\\label{prof:parameters}\n \\begin{gathered}\n R=5\\cdot10^{-10},\\qquad\n \\mathcal Z=\\{z_*\\in\\mathbb{R}^2:\\lVert z_*\\rVert_\\infty\\le R\\},\\qquad S_0=56,\\\\\n \\begin{pmatrix}k\\\\d\\end{pmatrix}\n =\\begin{pmatrix}-.094617116666041\\\\ .400682721856639\\end{pmatrix}\n +10^{-8}\n \\begin{pmatrix}25190&-63731\\\\721855&359157\\end{pmatrix}z_*.\n \\end{gathered}$$ Write $k_0,d_0$ for the central values. The square implies $$\\label{prof:parameter-displacement}\n |k-k_0|\\le4.44605\\cdot10^{-13},\\qquad\n |d-d_0|\\le5.40506\\cdot10^{-12}<.011R.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "claim-uniform-certified-finite-shooting",
      "kind": "bound",
      "quantifiers": [
        {

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Scalar-curvature convention** ([source](#eq:calabi-flow)): We use the scalar-curvature convention $S(\omega)=\operatorname{tr}_\omega\operatorname{Ric}(\omega)$.
- **Radial momentum coordinate and profile** ([source](#geo:reference)): For a radial potential $\Phi(\ell)$, its momentum coordinate and momentum profile are $$x=\Phi'(\ell),\qquad v(x)=\Phi''(\ell).$$
- **Legendre transform** ([source](#geo:reference)): The Legendre transform $u(x)=x\ell-\Phi(\ell)$ satisfies $u'=\ell$ and $u''=1/v$.
- **Momentum perturbation and curvature operators** ([source](#stmt-geo-reduction)): Define $$\label{geo:momentum} v=v_*+v_*^2H=v_*b,\qquad K(H)=w^{-1}(wv_*^2H)'',\qquad \mathcal AH=\big[w^{-1}(wv_*^2H)''\big]''.$$
- **Auxiliary sphere** ([source](#pm-h-6)): Set $$\label{geo:sphere} m=2n+4,\qquad \Sigma=S^{m+5}\subset\mathbb{R}^m\times\mathbb{R}^6,\qquad x=\|z\|^2.$$
- **Self-similar core** ([source](#geo:selfsimilar)): Then set $$\label{geo:selfsimilar} \rho=\sqrt{-t},\qquad y=x/\rho,\qquad h(y)=\frac{G(y)-1}{y},\qquad F(t,x)=\rho^{-1}h(x/\rho),\qquad t<0.$$
- **Full and spatial parabolic local norms** ([source](#ana:local-norm)): We use the following explicit version of the fourth-order parabolic norm: $$\label{ana:local-norm} \mathcal N_\alpha(u;D) =\sum_{j=0}^4\|D_z^ju\|_{\alpha;D}+\|u_t\|_{\alpha;D},\qquad \mathcal N'_\alpha(u;D)=\sum_{j=0}^4\|D_z^ju\|_{\alpha;D}.$$
- **Weighted local spaces** ([source](#stmt-ana-spaces)): For a fixed weight $p$, define $$\begin{aligned} \\|u\\|_{X_{\alpha,p}(s)} &=\sup_P\mathcal N_\alpha(s_0^{-p}u\circ\Psi_P;D_P),\label{ana:X}\\ \\|u\\|_{X'_{\alpha,p}(s)} &=\sup_P\mathcal N'_\alpha(s_0^{-p}u\circ\Psi_P;D_P),\label{ana:Xprime}\\ \\|f\\|_{Y_{\alpha,p}(s)} &=\sup_P\|s_0^{4-p}f\circ\Psi_P\|_{\alpha;D_P}. \label{ana:Y} \end{aligned}$$
- **Scaled background derivative notation** ([source](#pm-h-27)): In a box with center $(z_0,t_0)$ and center scale $b=\sigma(z_0,t_0)$, $O_{\mathrm{sc}}(b^q)$ means that, for every pair of nonnegative integers $j,k$, the spatial derivatives of order $j$ and time derivatives of order $k$ are bounded by $C_{j,k}b^{q-j-4k}$ on that box.

| q-compact-parametrix-error | Operator norm of the compact parametrix remainder | ≤ | $C\bigl(\beta^2+\eta^{(1-\alpha)/4}\bigr)$ | Small core radius and small elapsed time relative to the core scale. |

          "anchor": "geo:curvature-rate",
          "exact_quote": "Let $G\\in C^\\infty[0,\\infty)$ satisfy $G(0)=1$ and $G'(0)\\ne0$, and define $F$ by <a href=\"#geo:selfsimilar\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"geo:selfsimilar\">[geo:selfsimilar]</a>. Suppose that for some $T>0$ and $\\delta>0$ there is a smooth invariant solution $H=F+V$ of <a href=\"#geo:equation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"geo:equation\">[geo:equation]</a> on $(-T,0)\\times\\Sigma$ such that $1+v_*H>0$ and $$\\label{geo:correction-bound}\n V(t,0)=O(\\rho^{-1+\\delta})\\qquad(t\\uparrow0).$$ Then a smooth $U(10)$-invariant metric $\\omega_0\\in[\\omega_*]$ has maximal smooth Calabi-flow existence interval $[0,T_*)$, where $T_*=T/2<\\infty$. At the fixed affine origin $p$, the scalar curvature of this flow satisfies $$\\label{geo:curvature-rate}\n S(\\tau,p)=a(T_*-\\tau)^{-1/2}\n       +O\\big(1+(T_*-\\tau)^{(-1+\\delta)/2}\\big),\\qquad\n a=-110G'(0)\\ne0.$$ In particular $S(\\tau,p)\\sim a(T_*-\\tau)^{-1/2}$, and $a>0$ when $G'(0)<0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }
      ],

For the real parameters in <a href=\"#prof:parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:parameters\">[prof:parameters]</a>, $1+p>0$ on $0\\le s\\le1$.",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }
