<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-exact-geometric-reduction** [claim](#stmt-geo-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-reduction): Endpoint inversion of the Legendre transform, direct scalar-curvature calculation, and evolution of the affine Legendre terms. -> An exact normalized Calabi flow reconstructed from a solution of the reduced scalar equation.
- **step-regular-sphere-realization** [claim](#stmt-geo-operators) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-operators): Even-function endpoint regularity, spherical volume pushforward, and polynomial verification of the operator factorization. -> Smooth invariant sphere realization and the Euclidean biharmonic flat operator identity.
- **step-origin-analytic-family** [claim](#stmt-prof-origin) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prof-origin): Coefficient recurrence and contraction in a power-series Banach algebra. -> A unique holomorphic origin family with a uniform majorant and positive real initial segment.
- **step-root-and-projection-enclosures** [claim](#stmt-prof-root-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prof-root-bounds): Rational sign tests, Vieta identities, and interval evaluation of the projection and convolution expressions. -> Separated stable and growing roots with quantitative projection and Green-kernel bounds.
- **step-tail-residual-enclosure** [claim](#stmt-prof-tail-residual) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prof-tail-residual): Exact cancellation of the initial asymptotic powers followed by positive-coefficient majorants and parameter differentiation. -> Uniform residual and matching-jet parameter bounds.
- **step-certified-finite-shot** [claim](#stmt-cert-shoot) / [proof](#cert:appendix): Outward-rounded integer intervals, complex-disk Taylor remainders, physical transfer matrices, and a nonlinear parameter bootstrap. -> Uniform continuation, positivity, and matching-coordinate bounds for the complete shooting square.
- **step-tail-contraction** [claim](#stmt-prof-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prof-tail): Stable forward and growing backward Green integrals followed by a contraction on bounded sources. -> A continuously parameterized family of bounded positive tails with controlled growing coordinates.
- **step-topological-profile-matching** [claim](#stmt-prof-profile) / [proof](#pm-h-13): A continuous self-map of the shooting square and Brouwer's fixed point theorem. -> A global positive matched profile solution with negative origin slope.
- **step-weighted-remainder-identification** [claim](#stmt-prof-weighted-remainder) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prof-weighted-remainder): Formal coefficient recursion, bounded and weighted Green contractions, and uniqueness to identify the actual remainder. -> Differentiated polynomial remainder bounds at every fixed order.
- **step-complete-flat-shrinker** [claim](#stmt-prof-profile) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prof-weighted-remainder-2): Change of variables, chain-rule conversion of differentiated asymptotics, and the self-similar profile identity. -> The smooth positive shrinking profile with all stated asymptotics and its exact flat flow.
- **step-local-schauder-control** [claim](#stmt-ana-schauder) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ana-schauder): Frozen principal heat kernels, cancellation estimates, coefficient localization, and nested-domain absorption. -> Full local parabolic estimates with constants uniform down to a short initial face.
- **step-interior-energy-input** [claim](#stmt-ana-l2) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ana-l2): Averaging over preceding cylinders and interpolation replace the supremum input in the local estimate. -> Interior parabolic estimates requiring no terminal trace.
- **step-scale-uniform-forward-problem** [claim](#stmt-ana-short) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ana-short): Apply initial-face estimates in frozen-scale boxes and absorb the supremum using zero initial data. -> A bounded zero-data forward solution with an additional weighted supremum smallness factor.
- **step-short-time-spatial-interpolation** [claim](#stmt-ana-interpolation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ana-interpolation): Interpolate the small supremum against the uniform parabolic norm and use a higher Hölder exponent for the highest spatial derivatives. -> Small lower derivatives and small full spatial parabolic norm, with the time derivative omitted.
- **step-similarity-evolution-and-compactness** [claim](#stmt-flat-inverse) / [proof](#pm-h-22): A whole-space kernel parametrix constructs the evolution; translated limits and bounded-solution uniqueness prove compactness of its difference from the constant-coefficient evolution. -> A strongly continuous radial evolution with a compact time-one perturbation.
- **step-spectral-ancient-solution** [claim](#stmt-flat-inverse) / [proof](#pm-h-24): Analytic Fredholm theory selects a spectral circle, after which stable past and finite-dimensional future sums solve the forcing recursion. -> A real radial ancient solution with a temporal weighted supremum estimate and selected initial values.
- **step-full-flat-weighted-estimate** [claim](#stmt-flat-inverse) / [proof](#pm-h-25): Core Schauder estimates and rescaled annular estimates from a finite spacetime energy input recover the full spatial weight. -> The bounded flat right inverse in the full weighted parabolic norm.
- **step-scaled-background-mismatch** [claim](#stmt-glue-background) / [proof](#proof-stmt-glue-background): Differentiate the profile in core variables and its asymptotic expansion outside, then compare the compact and Euclidean spatial operators. -> Scaled background, geometric mismatch, and compact residual bounds.
- **step-extend-localized-inner-source** [claim](#stmt-glue-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-glue-extension): Extend the lower-face spatial trace with a scale-dependent temporal cutoff and control Hölder increments across the joining face. -> A bounded real radial source on the ancient flat cylinder.
- **step-compact-parametrix-and-inverse** [claim](#stmt-glue-inverse) / [proof](#proof-stmt-glue-inverse): Cancel the inner cutoff commutator in the outer forcing and invert the remaining small geometric and outer commutator errors by a Neumann series. -> A uniformly bounded compact right inverse with no prescribed initial values.
- **step-special-residual-smallness** [claim](#stmt-glue-special-error) / [proof](#proof-stmt-glue-special-error): Split the residual using the inner output cutoff, estimate the small core source, and apply higher-exponent spatial smallness to the exterior zero-data problem. -> An arbitrarily small corrected residual in the primed spatial parabolic norm.
- **step-nonlinear-compact-correction** [claim](#stmt-glue-solution) / [proof](#proof-stmt-glue-special-error-2): Contract the spatial nonlinear fixed-point map in the primed space, recover the time derivative through the inverse, and bootstrap interior smoothness. -> A positive smooth compact solution with a subleading origin correction.
- **step-curvature-obstruction** [claim](#stmt-geo-obstruction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-obstruction): Evaluate the exact scalar-curvature identity at the fixed origin, translate an interior time slice, and use smooth-flow uniqueness to identify the maximal lifetime. -> Finite maximal smooth lifetime with the stated scalar-curvature asymptotic.
- **step-main-counterexample** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-glue-special-error-3): Apply the curvature obstruction to the compact realization and take a smooth interior slice as the initial metric. -> The invariant finite-time singular Calabi flow in the Fubini–Study class.
- **step-products-with-stationary-factors** [claim](#stmt-cor-product-singularities) / [proof](#proof-stmt-glue-special-error-4): Add scalar curvatures and their averages for product metrics, apply smooth uniqueness, and scale projective stationary factors to a common Einstein constant. -> Product singularities with the same maximal time and examples in the stated higher dimensions.

        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-glue-special-error-2",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-glue-special-error</summary>\n\n\n\n\n\n*Proof of Theorem <a href=\"#glue:solution\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"glue:solution\">19</a>.* Expanding <a href=\"#glue:equation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:equation\">[glue:equation]</a> at $F$ gives $$\\label{glue:nonlinear-equation}\n \\mathcal PV+E_c+N(V)=0,$$ where $$\\label{glue:nonlinearity}\n N(V)=v_*^2V^2\\mathcal AF+\n       \\bigl(2v_*g_cV+v_*^2V^2\\bigr)\\mathcal AV.$$ This expression contains only spatial derivatives of $V$, so is defined on $X'_\\alpha$. For clarity, on a box of scale $b$, a function with $\\lVert V\\rVert_{X'_\\alpha}\\le\\varepsilon$ has scaled sizes $$V=O(\\varepsilon b^{-2+2\\delta}),\\qquad\n \\mathcal AV=O(\\varepsilon b^{-6+2\\delta}),$$ where these statements include the scaled parabolic $C^\\alpha$ norms. By Lemma <a href=\"#glue:background\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"glue:background\">20</a>, the two quadratic terms in <a href=\"#glue:nonlinearity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:nonlinearity\">[glue:nonlinearity]</a> therefore have size $C\\varepsilon^2b^{-6+4\\delta}$, and the cubic term has size $C\\varepsilon^3b^{-6+6\\delta}$. Relative to the $Y$ weight these gain $b^{2\\delta}$ and $b^{4\\delta}$, respectively. Since $b$ is bounded above on the compact domain, Hölder multiplication and the factorization of differences of products give uniform bounds $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\lVert N(V)\\rVert_{Y_\\alpha}&\\le C_N(\\varepsilon^2+\\varepsilon^3),\n \\label{glue:nonlinear-size}\\\\\n \\lVert N(V)-N(W)\\rVert_{Y_\\alpha}\n &\\le C_N(\\varepsilon+\\varepsilon^2)\\lVert V-W\\rVert_{X'_\\alpha}\n \\label{glue:nonlinear-lipschitz}\n\\end{aligned}$$ on the closed ball of radius $\\varepsilon$ in $X'_\\alpha$. The fixed sphere coefficients in $\\mathcal A$ have bounded rescaled norms, so these estimates apply on the whole sphere, including the other endpoint $x=1$.\n\nWe specify the parameter choices to avoid a dependence on an inverse norm that is still being chosen. First fix the profile, exponents, flat inverse, and the constants $\\beta_0,\\eta_0,C_D$ of Proposition <a href=\"#glue:inverse\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"glue:inverse\">22</a>. The nonlinear constant $C_N$ is uniform throughout this parameter range. Choose the requested ball radius $\\varepsilon>0$ so small that $$C_D C_N(\\varepsilon+\\varepsilon^2)\\le\\tfrac14,\\qquad\n C_*\\varepsilon\\le\\tfrac12\\mu,$$ where $C_*$ bounds $\\lvert v_*V\\rvert/\\lVert V\\rVert_{X'_\\alpha}$ uniformly. Such a $C_*$ exists because $v_*V$ has scaled size $C\\varepsilon b^{2\\delta}$ and $b\\le\\sqrt2$. Now take $\\beta\\le\\beta_0$ sufficiently small, and next $\\eta\\le\\min(\\eta_0,a_0^4)$ sufficiently small, that Lemma <a href=\"#glue:special-error\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"glue:special-error\">23</a> gives $\\lVert DE_c\\rVert_{X'_\\alpha}\\le\\varepsilon/2$; put $T=\\eta\\beta^4$.\n\nThe map $$\\mathcal T(V)=-DE_c-DN(V)$$ takes the closed $X'_\\alpha$ ball of radius $\\varepsilon$ into itself by <a href=\"#glue:nonlinear-size\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:nonlinear-size\">[glue:nonlinear-size]</a>, and is a contraction by <a href=\"#glue:nonlinear-lipschitz\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:nonlinear-lipschitz\">[glue:nonlinear-lipschitz]</a>. The real invariant subspace is closed, so the Banach fixed point theorem gives a real invariant fixed point there. Its fixed point identity also shows that this same $V$ belongs to $X_\\alpha$, because $E_c+N(V)\\in Y_\\alpha$ and $D:Y_\\alpha\\to X_\\alpha$. Applying $\\mathcal PD=I$ therefore yields <a href=\"#glue:nonlinear-equation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:nonlinear-equation\">[glue:nonlinear-equation]</a> in the classical Hölder sense and proves <a href=\"#glue:equation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:equation\">[glue:equation]</a>. This recovers the time derivative after the contraction has been performed.\n\nBy <a href=\"#glue:background-positive\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:background-positive\">[glue:background-positive]</a> and the radius choice, $$\\tfrac12\\mu\\le 1+v_*(F+V)\\le M+\\tfrac12\\mu,$$ which proves <a href=\"#glue:positive\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:positive\">[glue:positive]</a>. On each compact time subinterval of $(-T,0)$, the weight is bounded above and below, and the equation for $H$ is a uniformly parabolic scalar equation with principal multiplier $(1+v_*H)^2$. Its current regularity is $C^{4+\\alpha,1+\\alpha/4}$. Interior spatial difference quotients in this equation have the same principal operator; their sources are bounded in $C^\\alpha$ because the coefficient is a smooth function of $x,H$ and $H$ already has four spatial derivatives. The interior estimate of Lemma <a href=\"#ana:schauder\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ana:schauder\">13</a>, followed by passage to the difference-quotient limit on a smaller cylinder, gains one spatial derivative. Repetition gains every spatial derivative. The equation then gives corresponding spatial bounds for $H_t$; differentiating the equation in time and repeating the interior estimates gives all mixed derivatives. Thus $H$ is smooth on $\\Sigma\\times(-T,0)$. Invariant smoothness gives smoothness in $x\\in[0,1]$ by Lemma <a href=\"#geo:operators\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"geo:operators\">4</a>. At $x=0$, the $X'$ weight reads $$\\lvert V(t,0)\\rvert\\le\\varepsilon\\sigma(t,0)^{-2+2\\delta}\n                =\\varepsilon\\rho^{-1+\\delta},$$ up to the fixed equivalent-norm constant. This proves <a href=\"#glue:center\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:center\">[glue:center]</a> and finishes the construction. ◻",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }
      ],
      "reason": "The compact correction uses infinite-dimensional contraction and qualitative smallness choices after the spectral inverse has been selected; the source gives no finite certified representation of the resulting metric.",
      "step_id": "step-nonlinear-compact-correction"
    }
  ],
  "pm_id": "PM-352.1",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "geo:legendre-sign",
          "exact_quote": "Finally, fix $t_0\\in I$, choose $u(t_0)$ as above, and define at fixed momentum coordinate $$\\label{geo:legendre-flow}\n u(t,x)=u(t_0,x)+\\int_{t_0}^{t}K(H)(s,x)\\,\\mathrm{d}s.$$ Equation <a href=\"#geo:equation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"geo:equation\">[geo:equation]</a> implies $$\\partial_t(1/v)=-\\frac{v_*^2H_t}{v^2}\n =\\mathcal AH=\\partial_x^2K(H)=\\partial_tu''.$$ Their initial values agree, so $u''=1/v$ for all time. In particular the preceding metric construction applies throughout $I$. Differentiating the Legendre dual $\\Phi(t,\\ell)=x(t,\\ell)\\ell-u(t,x(t,\\ell))$ at fixed $\\ell$ cancels the terms containing $x_t$, and gives $$\\label{geo:legendre-sign}\n \\Phi_t=-u_t=-K(H)=S-\\bar S.$$ The difference between $\\Phi(t)$ and $\\Phi(t_0)$ is globally smooth by the endpoint argument and therefore supplies $\\phi$ in <a href=\"#geo:potential-flow\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"geo:potential-flow\">[geo:potential-flow]</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-exact-geometric-reduction",
      "inputs": [
        "Smooth momentum perturbation",
        "Positive momentum multiplier",
        "Fixed Fubini–Study class"
      ],
      "method": "Endpoint inversion of the Legendre transform, direct scalar-curvature calculation, and evolution of the affine Legendre terms.",
      "output": "An exact normalized Calabi flow reconstructed from a solution of the reduced scalar equation.",

          "anchor": "stmt-geo-operators",
          "exact_quote": "This identification is a bijection between $C^\\infty[0,1]$ and the smooth invariant functions on $\\Sigma$. The pushforward of spherical volume under $x$ is a positive constant multiple of $wv_*^2\\,\\mathrm{d}x$. With the nonpositive Laplacian convention, put $L=\\frac14\\Delta_\\Sigma$. On invariant functions, $$\\label{geo:sphere-operator}\n L=x(1-x)\\partial_x^2+\\big(n+2-(n+5)x\\big)\\partial_x,\n \\qquad\n \\mathcal A=(-L+n+3)(-L+2(n+2)).$$ The right-hand expression defines a smooth elliptic operator of order four on all of $\\Sigma$.*\n\n\n\n*In the core coordinates $(z,\\theta)\\mapsto(z,\\sqrt{1-|z|^2}\\,\\theta)$, with $\\theta\\in S^5$, its Laplacian on functions independent of $\\theta$ is $$\\label{geo:local-laplacian}\n \\Delta_\\Sigma\n =\\Delta_z-\\sum_{i,j=1}^m z_i z_j\\partial_{z_i z_j}\n             -(m+5)\\sum_{i=1}^m z_i\\partial_{z_i}.$$ The flat momentum operator obtained by replacing $v_*$ by $x$ in <a href=\"#geo:momentum\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"geo:momentum\">[geo:momentum]</a> satisfies $$\\label{geo:flat-operator}\n \\widehat\\mathcal AH\n :=\\big[x^{1-n}(x^{n+1}H)''\\big]''\n =\\big(x\\partial_x^2+(n+2)\\partial_x\\big)^2H\n =\\frac1{16}\\Delta_{\\mathbb{R}^m}^2H,\\qquad x=|z|^2.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-regular-sphere-realization",
      "inputs": [
        "Momentum coordinate",
        "Auxiliary sphere",
        "Compact momentum operator"
      ],
      "method": "Even-function endpoint regularity, spherical volume pushforward, and polynomial verification of the operator factorization.",

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-geo-operators"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prof-origin",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "prof:origin-recurrence",
          "exact_quote": "In the variable $r=s^{3/2}$, <a href=\"#prof:ode\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:ode\">[prof:ode]</a> is equivalent to $$Q(r\\partial_r)(p-d)=\\frac8{27}r^2\\bigl((1+p)^{-1}-1\\bigr).$$ Consequently the coefficients of an analytic solution must satisfy $$\\label{prof:origin-recurrence}\n a_i=\n \\frac{[r^{i-2}]\\bigl((1+p)^{-1}-1\\bigr)}\n {(27/8)(i-1)(i+9)(i+10)},\\qquad i\\ge2.$$ The constant and linear equations leave $d,k$ free, and this recurrence determines every subsequent coefficient uniquely.",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-origin-analytic-family",
      "inputs": [
        "Transformed profile equation",
        "Free origin parameters",
        "Complex parameter polydisc"
      ],
      "method": "Coefficient recurrence and contraction in a power-series Banach algebra.",
      "output": "A unique holomorphic origin family with a uniform majorant and positive real initial segment.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prof-origin"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prof-root-bounds",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prof-root-bounds",
          "exact_quote": "The roots in <a href=\"#prof:coordinates\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:coordinates\">[prof:coordinates]</a> are distinct and satisfy $$\\label{prof:root-boxes}\n \\begin{split}\n -1.08659&<\\lambda_0<-1.08655,\\\\\n .40933&<\\Re\\lambda_1<.40938,\\qquad\n .8671<\\Im\\lambda_1<.86721.\n \\end{split}$$ Every entry of $\\Gamma-\\Lambda$ has modulus less than $.001$. The absolute row sums of $\\Gamma$ are less than $.92,.68,.68$, respectively. With $$\\label{prof:convolution-constants}\n M_i=\\sum_{j=0}^2\n \\frac{|\\lambda_j|^i}{|\\Re\\lambda_j|\\,|D_0'(\\lambda_j)|},\\qquad 0\\le i\\le2,$$ one has $$M_0<1.96,\\qquad M_1<1.92,\\qquad M_2<1.88,\n \\qquad \\frac1{\\Re\\lambda_1|D_0'(\\lambda_1)|}<.82.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-root-and-projection-enclosures",
      "inputs": [
        "Constant-coefficient comparison polynomial",
        "Matching eigenbasis",
        "Rational projection approximation"
      ],
      "method": "Rational sign tests, Vieta identities, and interval evaluation of the projection and convolution expressions.",
      "output": "Separated stable and growing roots with quantitative projection and Green-kernel bounds.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prof-root-bounds"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prof-tail-residual",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "prof:residual-arithmetic",
          "exact_quote": "$$\\label{prof:residual-arithmetic}\n \\sum_{j>4}[z^j]B_*(z)^2<8\\cdot10^{-14},\\qquad\n \\sum_{j>4}[z^j]B_*(z)^3<2\\cdot10^{-14},\\qquad\n \\sum_{j>4}[z^j]B_*(z)^4<3\\cdot10^{-15}.$$ Since $B_*(1)<.001$, all powers at least five contribute at most $.001^5/(1-.001)$. The remaining linear term contributes at most $(243/4)(3.2\\cdot10^{-11})/56^3$. Thus the residual is bounded by $$8\\cdot10^{-14}+2\\cdot10^{-14}+3\\cdot10^{-15}\n +\\frac{.001^5}{1-.001}\n +\\frac{(243/4)(3.2\\cdot10^{-11})}{56^3}\n <3\\cdot10^{-13}.$$ These bounds hold for all $s\\ge56$ because every remaining absolute monomial decreases there. The residual is a rational function of $s^{-3}$, analytic at zero with vanishing constant term, which also proves the assertion about its derivatives. For the parameter estimate, the factors $3j/56$ and $3j(3j+1)/56^2$ are at most one for $1\\le j\\le4$. Thus the second row of the coefficient table gives $$\\lVert \\partial_dP_{\\rm a}\\rVert_\\infty\n \\le\\frac{304}{56^3}+\\frac{100000}{56^6}\n       +\\frac{65000000}{56^9}+\\frac{100000000000}{56^{12}}\n <.001735<.01.$$ The mean value theorem yields <a href=\"#prof:tail-parameter-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:tail-parameter-bound\">[prof:tail-parameter-bound]</a>. ◻",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }
      ],

      "output": "Uniform residual and matching-jet parameter bounds.",

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-cert-shoot",

        {
          "anchor": "cert:bootstrap-improvement",
          "exact_quote": "$$\\begin{aligned}\n |\\delta p|\n &\\le4.3(2.72+.11)R\n      +4.3\\frac{1.001(14R)^2}{.23^3}\\notag\\\\\n &<12.169035R<14R.\n \\label{cert:bootstrap-improvement}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here replacing the remaining block length by $1$ is a harmless overestimate. This improves the bootstrap strictly both at block starts and in block interiors, including the reference jumps. At $s=1$ it starts from $\\|Z\\|<.02$ and <a href=\"#cert:initial-error\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cert:initial-error\">[cert:initial-error]</a>. Continuation over the finitely many blocks therefore proves <a href=\"#cert:endpoint-error\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cert:endpoint-error\">[cert:endpoint-error]</a> at every block endpoint.",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "cert:conclusion",
          "exact_quote": "For every parameter $z_*\\in[-R,R]^2$, where $R=5\\cdot10^{-10}$, let $(k,d)$ be given by <a href=\"#prof:parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:parameters\">[prof:parameters]</a>, and let $p$ be the origin solution of <a href=\"#prof:ode\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:ode\">[prof:ode]</a> supplied by Lemma <a href=\"#prof:origin\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prof:origin\">7</a>. This solution extends to $0\\le s\\le S_0=56$ and satisfies $$1+p(s)>0.$$ For the coordinates of its jet relative to $p_{\\rm a}$, defined in <a href=\"#prof:coordinates\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:coordinates\">[prof:coordinates]</a>, one has $$\\label{cert:conclusion}\n |q|\\le .25R,\\qquad\n \\big\\|(\\Re\\xi,\\Im\\xi)-z_*\\big\\|_\\infty\\le .13R.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }
      ],

      "method": "Outward-rounded integer intervals, complex-disk Taylor remainders, physical transfer matrices, and a nonlinear parameter bootstrap.",

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prof-tail",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-root-and-projection-enclosures",

      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "prof:green-formula",
          "exact_quote": "Let $b_i=D_0'(\\lambda_i)^{-1}$ and let $j$ be a bounded continuous real function on $[S_0,\\infty)$. Define a vector $\\mathcal V=(V,V',V'')^t$ by the convergent integrals $$\\label{prof:green-formula}\n \\begin{split}\n \\mathcal V(s)={}&q\\mathrm{e}^{\\lambda_0(s-S_0)}v_0\n  +b_0v_0\\int_{S_0}^s\\mathrm{e}^{\\lambda_0(s-u)}j(u)\\,\\mathrm{d}u\\\\\n &-\\sum_{i=1}^2 b_iv_i\\int_s^\\infty\\mathrm{e}^{\\lambda_i(s-u)}j(u)\\,\\mathrm{d}u.\n \\end{split}$$ Indeed this vector solves the companion system for $D_0(\\partial_s)V=j$: the identities $\\sum b_i=\\sum\\lambda_ib_i=0$ and $\\sum\\lambda_i^2b_i=1$ follow by partial fractions of $1/D_0$. They show both the derivative relations between its entries and the source in the last component. The vector is real by complex conjugation. It is the unique bounded solution with the prescribed real-mode coordinate at $S_0$, since a bounded homogeneous solution has no growing nonreal modes.",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "prof:tail-contraction-constant",
          "exact_quote": "$$\\label{prof:tail-contraction-constant}\n \\frac{15}{56}(1.88)+\\frac{B}{2\\cdot56^2}(1.92)\n +\\left(\\frac{|C|}{2\\cdot56^3}+.0021\\right)(1.96)<.57.$$ The corresponding coefficient of the homogeneous term is bounded by $$\\label{prof:tail-homogeneous-constant}\n \\frac{15}{56}(1.08659)^2+\\frac{B}{2\\cdot56^2}(1.08659)\n +\\frac{|C|}{2\\cdot56^3}+.0021<.36.$$ Consequently $$\\lVert \\mathcal T_{q,d}j\\rVert_\\infty\n \\le3\\cdot10^{-13}+.36|q|+.57\\lVert j\\rVert_\\infty\n \\le(.0006+.36\\cdot.26+.57\\cdot.3)R=.2652R<.3R.$$ The contraction theorem gives a unique fixed point in this ball. Equation <a href=\"#prof:green-formula\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:green-formula\">[prof:green-formula]</a> then gives a $C^3$ solution, and <a href=\"#prof:ode\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:ode\">[prof:ode]</a> bootstraps it to a smooth solution for $s>0$. Its nonreal initial coordinate satisfies $$\\xi_{\\rm tail}=-b_1\\int_{S_0}^\\infty\n                  \\mathrm{e}^{\\lambda_1(S_0-u)}j(u)\\,\\mathrm{d}u,\n \\qquad |\\xi_{\\rm tail}|<.82(.3R)=.246R<.25R.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-tail-contraction",
      "inputs": [
        "Root and convolution bounds",
        "Small asymptotic residual",
        "Prescribed stable coordinate"
      ],
      "method": "Stable forward and growing backward Green integrals followed by a contraction on bounded sources.",
      "output": "A continuously parameterized family of bounded positive tails with controlled growing coordinates.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prof-tail"

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prof-profile",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-certified-finite-shot",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-13",
          "exact_quote": "#### Matching the two solutions\n\nFor a parameter $z_*\\in\\mathcal Z$, apply Lemma <a href=\"#prof:tail\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prof:tail\">10</a> using the value $d(z_*)$ and the coordinate $q_{\\rm shot}(z_*)$ supplied by the finite shot. This is allowed by $|q_{\\rm shot}|\\le .25R<.26R$. Define a map on the closed square by $$\\mathcal B(z_*)=z_*\n -\\bigl(\\Re(\\xi_{\\rm shot}-\\xi_{\\rm tail}),\n        \\Im(\\xi_{\\rm shot}-\\xi_{\\rm tail})\\bigr).$$ It is continuous by the continuous dependence established above and satisfies $$\\lVert \\mathcal B(z_*)\\rVert_\\infty\\le(.13+.25)R=.38R<R.$$ Brouwer’s fixed point theorem gives $z_*\\in\\mathcal Z$ with $\\xi_{\\rm shot}=\\xi_{\\rm tail}$. The real coordinate and the parameter $d$ already agree. The three jet entries in <a href=\"#prof:coordinates\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:coordinates\">[prof:coordinates]</a> therefore agree, and ordinary uniqueness for <a href=\"#prof:ode\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:ode\">[prof:ode]</a> joins the shot and tail to one solution on $[0,\\infty)$.\n\nThe finite shot has $1+p>0$, and the tail has $|p|<.001$. Compactness of the finite interval and the uniform tail bound imply that $1+p$ is bounded and bounded away from zero globally. Since $1+d>0$, the function $G$ in <a href=\"#prof:change-variables\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:change-variables\">[prof:change-variables]</a> has the same properties. Near zero, $$s^{3/2}=\\frac{\\sqrt{27}}{4c}y,\n \\qquad G(y)=1+\\frac{\\sqrt{27}}4ky+O(y^2).$$ It is therefore analytic there, with $G'(0)=k\\sqrt{27}/4<0$ by <a href=\"#prof:parameter-displacement\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:parameter-displacement\">[prof:parameter-displacement]</a>. This proves all assertions of Theorem <a href=\"#prof:profile\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prof:profile\">6</a> except for the differentiable expansion, which we now establish independently of the finite shooting estimates.",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-topological-profile-matching",
      "inputs": [
        "Certified finite-shot coordinates",
        "Continuous tail family",
        "Agreement of the stable coordinate and profile parameter"
      ],
      "method": "A continuous self-map of the shooting square and Brouwer's fixed point theorem.",

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prof-weighted-remainder",
      "depends_on": [

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "prof:weighted-source",
          "exact_quote": "Choose $S\\ge2N$ so late that $2\\sup_{s\\ge S}(|a_0|+|a_1|+|a_2|)<1$. The affine equation for the source, $$\\label{prof:weighted-source}\n j=-e_J-\\sum_{i=0}^2a_i\\bigl(H_i+(\\mathcal K_Sj)_i\\bigr),$$ is a contraction both in the bounded norm and in the norm $\\lVert \\cdot\\rVert_N$, by <a href=\"#prof:weighted-green\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:weighted-green\">[prof:weighted-green]</a>–<a href=\"#prof:weighted-green-2\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:weighted-green-2\">[prof:weighted-green-2]</a>. It has a unique solution in each space. The weighted solution is bounded, so uniqueness in the bounded space identifies the two solutions. On the other hand, the actual remainder has a bounded jet and bounded source $j=W'''+W$. The growing-mode integrals from infinity are forced by boundedness, so its jet has the representation $H+\\mathcal K_Sj$ with the chosen $q_S$. It satisfies <a href=\"#prof:weighted-source\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:weighted-source\">[prof:weighted-source]</a> and hence equals the weighted solution. We have proved $$\\label{prof:preliminary-remainder}\n W,W',W''=O(s^{-N}).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-weighted-remainder-identification",
      "inputs": [
        "Matched bounded profile jet",
        "Finite asymptotic truncation",
        "Decaying remainder-equation coefficients"
      ],
      "method": "Formal coefficient recursion, bounded and weighted Green contractions, and uniqueness to identify the actual remainder.",
      "output": "Differentiated polynomial remainder bounds at every fixed order.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prof-weighted-remainder"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prof-profile",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-weighted-remainder-identification"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-prof-weighted-remainder-2",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-prof-weighted-remainder</summary>\n\n*Completion of the proof of Theorem <a href=\"#prof:profile\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prof:profile\">6</a>.* Finally $s^{-3}=(16/27)c^2y^{-2}$, so <a href=\"#prof:s-expansion\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:s-expansion\">[prof:s-expansion]</a> and the chain rule give <a href=\"#prof:expansion\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:expansion\">[prof:expansion]</a>, with $g_j=c\\,d_j((16/27)c^2)^j$. Repeated differentiation of $u\\mapsto G(u^{-1/2})$ expresses each derivative as a finite sum of powers of $y$ times derivatives of $G$. Subtracting sufficiently many terms in <a href=\"#prof:expansion\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prof:expansion\">[prof:expansion]</a> shows that these derivatives have their Taylor limits at $u=0$. The resulting extension is $C^\\infty$. The origin expansion gives the smooth extension of $h$, and the calculation in Section <a href=\"#geo:core-section\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"geo:core-section\">2.2</a> proves the flat equation in Theorem <a href=\"#prof:profile\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prof:profile\">6</a>, also at $x=0$ by smoothness. ◻",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-complete-flat-shrinker",
      "inputs": [
        "Positive matched profile",
        "Differentiated asymptotic expansion",
        "Origin analytic expansion"
      ],
      "method": "Change of variables, chain-rule conversion of differentiated asymptotics, and the self-similar profile identity.",
      "output": "The smooth positive shrinking profile with all stated asymptotics and its exact flat flow.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prof-weighted-remainder-2"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-ana-schauder",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "ana:kernel-moment",
          "exact_quote": "We give the kernel and localization argument, particularly its uniformity in <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span>. For a frozen homogeneous principal operator $A_0$, with symbol $p(\\xi)\\ge\\lambda|\\xi|^4$, its heat kernel is $$k_b(z)=(2\\pi)^{-d}\\int_{\\mathbb{R}^d}e^{iz\\cdot\\xi-bp(\\xi)}\\,\\mathrm{d}\\xi,\n \\qquad b>0.$$ Fourier differentiation and scaling give, uniformly for the allowed symbols and every $N,j,\\ell$, $$\\label{ana:kernel}\n |D^j\\partial_b^\\ell k_b(z)|\n \\le C_{N,j,\\ell}b^{-(d+j)/4-\\ell}\n                  (1+|z|b^{-1/4})^{-N}.$$ Moreover $\\int k_b=1$ and $\\int D^jk_b=0$ for $j>0$. For $u(t)=\\int_0^tk_{t-s}*f(s)\\,\\mathrm{d}s$ and $j=4$, subtract $f(s,z)$ inside the convolution. Writing $F=\\sup_s[f(s)]_{\\alpha;\\mathbb{R}^d}$, $$\\label{ana:kernel-moment}\n \\|D^4k_b*f(s)\\|_\\infty\\le CF b^{-1+\\alpha/4},\\qquad\n \\|\\partial_bD^4k_b*f(s)\\|_\\infty\n                    \\le CF b^{-2+\\alpha/4}.$$ This proves $\\|D^4u(t)\\|_\\infty\\le CFt^{\\alpha/4}$. For a spatial increment of size $q$, split the lag integral at $q^4$. The short-lag part is bounded by $CF\\int_0^{q^4}b^{-1+\\alpha/4}\\,\\mathrm{d}b$. For the long-lag part use the mean value theorem and $\\|D^5k_b*f(s)\\|_\\infty\\le CFb^{(\\alpha-5)/4}$. Its bound is $CFq\\int_{q^4}^h b^{(\\alpha-5)/4}\\,\\mathrm{d}b$. Both are at most $CFq^\\alpha$, with an empty integral omitted.",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "ana:freeze-bound",
          "exact_quote": "$$\\label{ana:freeze-bound}\n |(a_\\nu^0-a_\\nu)D^\\nu u|_\\alpha\n \\le CKq^\\alpha|D^4u|_\\alpha\n             +CK\\|D^4u\\|_\\infty,$$ with fixed cutoff factors understood. First make $q$ small enough to absorb the first coefficient. Apply <a href=\"#ana:fourth-sup-interp\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:fourth-sup-interp\">[ana:fourth-sup-interp]</a> to the second term. For the lower-order terms apply <a href=\"#ana:interp-basic\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:interp-basic\">[ana:interp-basic]</a>, and use the equation only in the form $$\\label{ana:ut-sup}\n \\|u_t\\|_\\infty\\le\\|f\\|_\\infty\n                   +K\\sum_{j=0}^4\\|D^ju\\|_\\infty.$$ Thus no unknown temporal Hölder seminorm of $u_t$ is used to absorb a temporal seminorm of $D^4u$.",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-local-schauder-control",
      "inputs": [
        "Uniform ellipticity",
        "Parabolic Hölder coefficient bounds",
        "Interior or zero-data initial cylinders"
      ],
      "method": "Frozen principal heat kernels, cancellation estimates, coefficient localization, and nested-domain absorption.",

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-ana-l2",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ana-l2",
          "exact_quote": "Let $U_1\\Subset U_2$ be fixed bounded spatial domains and $b_0<b_1<b$, with $b-b_1$ bounded above. Suppose that the coefficient assumptions of Lemma <a href=\"#ana:schauder\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ana:schauder\">13</a> hold on $U_2\\times(b_0,b)$ and that $\\mathcal Lu=f$ there. Then $$\\label{ana:l2-estimate}\n \\mathcal N_\\alpha(u;U_1\\times[b_1,b))\n \\le C\\bigl(|f|_{\\alpha;U_2\\times(b_0,b)}\n                  +\\|u\\|_{L^2(U_2\\times(b_0,b))}\\bigr).$$ The constant depends on the spatial gap, $b_1-b_0$, the upper bound for $b-b_1$, and the coefficient constants, but requires no terminal trace. The estimate is unchanged if the upper face is included and the solution is defined there. In particular it holds on fixed nested annuli through an open upper time endpoint.",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }
      ],

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-ana-l2"
    },

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-ana-short",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "ana:short-bounds",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $p\\in\\mathbb{R}$, $0<\\alpha<1$, and the preceding scale and coefficient constants. There exist $\\eta_0>0$ and $C<\\infty$, independent of $\\inf s$, with the following property. If $$0<T\\le\\eta_0(\\inf s)^4,\\qquad\n \\mathcal Lu=f\\quad\\hbox{on }M\\times[0,T],\\qquad u(\\cdot,0)=0,$$ then, with $\\eta=T/(\\inf s)^4$, $$\\label{ana:short-bounds}\n \\|u\\|_{X_{\\alpha,p}(s)}\\le C\\|f\\|_{Y_{\\alpha,p}(s)},\\qquad\n \\sup_{z,t}s(z)^{-p}|u(z,t)|\n                         \\le C\\eta\\|f\\|_{Y_{\\alpha,p}(s)}.$$ For each fixed positive scale and smooth coefficients the zero-data Cauchy problem has a unique solution with this regularity for $f\\in Y_{\\alpha,p}(s)$. The same estimates hold on every interval $[0,T')$, $T'\\le T$, on which the coefficients and source obey the stated bounds.",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }
      ],

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-ana-short"

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-ana-interpolation",
      "depends_on": [

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "ana:lower-small",
          "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Lemma <a href=\"#ana:short\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ana:short\">15</a>, the local scaled parabolic $C^\\alpha$ norms of the spatial derivatives of order $0\\le j\\le3$, with weight $p-j$, satisfy $$\\label{ana:lower-small}\n \\sup_P\\bigl|D_y^j(s_0^{-p}u\\circ\\Psi_P)\\bigr|_{\\alpha;D_P}\n \\le C\\eta^{(4-j-\\alpha)/4}\\|f\\|_{Y_{\\alpha,p}(s)}.$$ If the coefficient and source bounds also hold at an exponent $\\alpha'\\in(\\alpha,1)$, then $$\\label{ana:fourth-small}\n \\|u\\|_{X'_{\\alpha,p}(s)}\n \\le C\\eta^{(\\alpha'-\\alpha)/4}\n                    \\|f\\|_{Y_{\\alpha',p}(s)}.$$ Constants may depend on $\\alpha'$ but remain independent of the scale and elapsed time.",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "ana:fourth-small",
          "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Lemma <a href=\"#ana:short\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ana:short\">15</a>, the local scaled parabolic $C^\\alpha$ norms of the spatial derivatives of order $0\\le j\\le3$, with weight $p-j$, satisfy $$\\label{ana:lower-small}\n \\sup_P\\bigl|D_y^j(s_0^{-p}u\\circ\\Psi_P)\\bigr|_{\\alpha;D_P}\n \\le C\\eta^{(4-j-\\alpha)/4}\\|f\\|_{Y_{\\alpha,p}(s)}.$$ If the coefficient and source bounds also hold at an exponent $\\alpha'\\in(\\alpha,1)$, then $$\\label{ana:fourth-small}\n \\|u\\|_{X'_{\\alpha,p}(s)}\n \\le C\\eta^{(\\alpha'-\\alpha)/4}\n                    \\|f\\|_{Y_{\\alpha',p}(s)}.$$ Constants may depend on $\\alpha'$ but remain independent of the scale and elapsed time.",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }
      ],

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-flat-inverse",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-complete-flat-shrinker",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "flat:volterra",
          "exact_quote": "For $d\\in C_0$ and $l\\in C([0,1];C_0)$ solve $$\\label{flat:volterra}\n \\begin{split}\n j(s)+\\int_0^sJ_{s,r}j(r)\\,\\mathrm{d}r&=l(s)-J_{s,0}d,\\\\\n u(s)&=K_{s,0}d+\\int_0^sK_{s,r}j(r)\\,\\mathrm{d}r.\n \\end{split}$$ All integrals are Bochner integrals. The inverse of the first equation is its Volterra series. For completeness, if $H(s)=Cs^{-3/4}$, its $k$-fold convolution on the positive half-line is $$H^{*k}(s)\n =\\frac{C^k\\Gamma(1/4)^k}{\\Gamma(k/4)}s^{k/4-1}\n \\quad(k\\ge1).$$ This identity follows by the beta integral and induction. Since the right side of the first equation in <a href=\"#flat:volterra\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"flat:volterra\">[flat:volterra]</a> is bounded by $Cs^{-3/4}(\\|d\\|_\\infty+\\|l\\|_{C([0,1];C_0)})$, the same formula and the growth of the gamma function prove convergence of the series and the estimates $$\\label{flat:evolution-bound}\n \\|j(s)\\|_\\infty\\le Cs^{-3/4}\n       (\\|d\\|_\\infty+\\|l\\|_{C([0,1];C_0)}),\\qquad\n \\sup_{0\\le s\\le1}\\|u(s)\\|_\\infty\n \\le C(\\|d\\|_\\infty+\\|l\\|_{C([0,1];C_0)}).$$ The integral in the formula for $u$ tends to zero as $O(s^{1/4})$. Together with <a href=\"#flat:approximate-identity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"flat:approximate-identity\">[flat:approximate-identity]</a>, this proves $u\\in C([0,1];C_0)$ and $u(0)=d$. Continuity at positive times follows by dominated convergence, splitting off a short interval adjacent to the diagonal.",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "flat:compact-difference",
          "exact_quote": "Write $P=S(1)|_E$, reserving this symbol here for a bounded operator, and let $P_c$ be the corresponding operator with $A_f=c^2$ and $B_f=0$. We prove $$\\label{flat:compact-difference}\n P-P_c:E\\longrightarrow E\\quad\\hbox{is compact}.$$ Both time-one evolutions map their unit balls to locally equicontinuous functions by the interior estimates. It remains to prove uniform decay of their difference at spatial infinity.",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-similarity-evolution-and-compactness",
      "inputs": [
        "Smooth positive shrinker",
        "Decay of similarity coefficients",
        "Local parabolic regularity"
      ],
      "method": "A whole-space kernel parametrix constructs the evolution; translated limits and bounded-solution uniqueness prove compactness of its difference from the constant-coefficient evolution.",
      "output": "A strongly continuous radial evolution with a compact time-one perturbation.",
      "proof_anchor": "pm-h-22"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-flat-inverse",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-similarity-evolution-and-compactness"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "flat:weight-choice",
          "exact_quote": "$$\\label{flat:weight-choice}\n e^{-1/2}<q<1,\\qquad\n \\{|\\lambda|=q\\}\\cap\\mathop{\\mathrm{spec}}(P)=\\varnothing,\\qquad\n \\gamma=-\\log q\\in(0,\\tfrac12),\\qquad \\delta=2\\gamma.$$ Such a circle exists by the discreteness just proved. There are only finitely many eigenvalues outside it: they lie in a bounded closed annulus contained in $\\Omega$. Let $\\Pi_u$ be their summed Riesz projection, defined by resolvent integration around these eigenvalues, and put $\\Pi_s=I-\\Pi_u$. The resolvent identity gives commuting projections and the invariant decomposition $$E=E_s\\oplus E_u,\\qquad E_s=\\Pi_sE,\\quad E_u=\\Pi_uE,\n \\qquad P_s=P|_{E_s},\\quad P_u=P|_{E_u}.$$ The space $E_u$ is finite dimensional, $P_u$ is invertible, and the two restricted spectra lie strictly inside and strictly outside the chosen circle, respectively. If $E_u=\\{0\\}$, all terms involving it below are omitted. Compactness of the restricted spectra and the spectral radius formula give numbers $r_s<q<r_u$ such that $$\\label{flat:dichotomy-bounds}\n \\|P_s^k\\|\\le C r_s^k,\\qquad\n \\|P_u^{-k}\\|\\le C r_u^{-k},\\qquad k\\ge0.$$ For example choose $r_s$ strictly between $r(P_s)$ and $q$, and $r_u$ strictly between $q$ and the smallest modulus of an eigenvalue of $P_u$; the spectral radius formula controls all sufficiently large powers, and their finitely many predecessors are absorbed in $C$. The projections commute with complex conjugation because the enclosed spectral sets are invariant under conjugation.",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "flat:recursion-solution",
          "exact_quote": "Let the one-step source increments be $$l_j=\\int_0^1S(1-s)f^\\#(j+s)\\,\\mathrm{d}s,\\qquad j\\in\\mathbb{Z}.$$ Then $\\|l_j\\|_E\\le CMq^j$. Define $$\\label{flat:recursion-solution}\n v_j=\\sum_{k=0}^\\infty P_s^k\\Pi_s l_{j-1-k}\n       -\\sum_{k=0}^\\infty P_u^{-k-1}\\Pi_u l_{j+k}.$$ Both series converge absolutely for every integer $j$. More explicitly, their norms are at most $$CMq^j\\left[\n   q^{-1}\\sum_{k=0}^\\infty(r_s/q)^k\n       +r_u^{-1}\\sum_{k=0}^\\infty(q/r_u)^k\\right]\n \\le C'Mq^j.$$ Shifting the summation indices proves $v_{j+1}=Pv_j+l_j$. On $[j,j+1]$ define $v$ by <a href=\"#flat:duhamel\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"flat:duhamel\">[flat:duhamel]</a> with initial value $v_j$. The definitions agree at the endpoints and give $$\\label{flat:preliminary-bound}\n \\|v(\\tau)\\|_E\\le CM e^{-\\gamma\\tau},\\qquad\n |V(z,t)|\\le CM\\rho^{-1+\\delta}.$$ The continuous joining creates no distributional time-boundary term, so <a href=\"#flat:similarity-equation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"flat:similarity-equation\">[flat:similarity-equation]</a> holds across every joined face. Interior regularity makes it a classical equation there. The construction is linear and real on real sources. The second sum in <a href=\"#flat:recursion-solution\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"flat:recursion-solution\">[flat:recursion-solution]</a> selects the outside spectral components using future forcing; its use is permitted precisely because no initial values have been imposed.",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-spectral-ancient-solution",
      "inputs": [
        "Compact evolution perturbation",
        "Damped constant-coefficient evolution",
        "Weighted ancient source"
      ],
      "method": "Analytic Fredholm theory selects a spectral circle, after which stable past and finite-dimensional future sums solve the forcing recursion.",
      "output": "A real radial ancient solution with a temporal weighted supremum estimate and selected initial values.",
      "proof_anchor": "pm-h-24"
    },

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-flat-inverse",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-spectral-ancient-solution",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "flat:annular-l2",
          "exact_quote": "$$\\label{flat:annular-l2}\n \\|U\\|_{L^2(\\mathcal U\\times(-4,0))}^2\n \\le C M^2|\\mathcal U|\\int_0^4 q^{-1+\\delta}\\,\\mathrm{d}q\n =C M^2|\\mathcal U|\\frac{4^\\delta}{\\delta}<\\infty.$$ The value of $\\delta$ has already been fixed, so the dependence on $1/\\delta$ is harmless.",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "flat:annular-full",
          "exact_quote": "Apply Lemma <a href=\"#ana:l2\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ana:l2\">14</a> to <a href=\"#flat:annular-equation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"flat:annular-equation\">[flat:annular-equation]</a> on nested annuli, with the preceding-time buffer from $-4$ to $-2$. It gives $$\\label{flat:annular-full}\n \\sum_{j=0}^4\\|D_y^jU\\|_{C^\\alpha(\\mathcal U'\\times(-2,0))}\n +\\|U_s\\|_{C^\\alpha(\\mathcal U'\\times(-2,0))}\n \\le CM.$$ One first applies the estimate with terminal time $b<0$. On each such truncated slab $U$ has finite supremum norm by <a href=\"#flat:preliminary-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"flat:preliminary-bound\">[flat:preliminary-bound]</a>; the coefficient, source, and $L^2$ bounds above are independent of $b$. Letting $b\\uparrow0$ then proves <a href=\"#flat:annular-full\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"flat:annular-full\">[flat:annular-full]</a>, including Hölder quotients between times arbitrarily close to zero. The estimate uses preceding times and requires no terminal value at $s=0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-full-flat-weighted-estimate",
      "inputs": [

      "method": "Core Schauder estimates and rescaled annular estimates from a finite spacetime energy input recover the full spatial weight.",

      "proof_anchor": "pm-h-25"

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-glue-background",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-glue-background",
          "exact_quote": "For $0<T\\le1$, on the compact domain one has $$\\label{glue:background-bounds}\n F=O_{\\mathrm{sc}}(b^{-2}),\\qquad\n \\mathcal AF=O_{\\mathrm{sc}}(b^{-6}),\\qquad\n A_c=O_{\\mathrm{sc}}(1),\\qquad\n B_c=O_{\\mathrm{sc}}(b^{-4}),\\qquad\n v_*=O_{\\mathrm{sc}}(b^2).$$ The analogous flat bounds hold in a fixed neighborhood of $r=0$. In the common coordinates there, write $$\\mathcal P-\\widehat\\mathcal P=\\sum_{|\\nu|\\le4}d_\\nu(z,t)\\partial_z^\\nu.$$ For each $j,k\\ge0$, the rescaled coefficients satisfy $$\\label{glue:mismatch-coeff}\n b^{4-|\\nu|+j+4k}\n \\lvert \\partial_z^j\\partial_t^k d_\\nu\\rvert\\le C_{j,k}b^2.$$ Finally, near $r=0$, $$\\label{glue:error-order}\n E_c=O_{\\mathrm{sc}}(b^{-4}).$$ Away from a fixed neighborhood of $r=0$, all these background quantities extend smoothly to $t=0$ with bounds of every finite order.",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-scaled-background-mismatch",
      "inputs": [
        "Flat profile with differentiated asymptotics",
        "Sphere coordinate operator",
        "Compact and flat positivity multipliers"
      ],
      "method": "Differentiate the profile in core variables and its asymptotic expansion outside, then compare the compact and Euclidean spatial operators.",
      "output": "Scaled background, geometric mismatch, and compact residual bounds.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-glue-background"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-glue-extension",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "glue:extension-formula",
          "exact_quote": "$$\\label{glue:extension-formula}\n (\\mathcal E_i f)(t,z)\n =f_i(-T,z)\\,\\psi\\left(\\frac{-t-T}{(|z|^2+\\sqrt T)^2}\\right).$$ Let $b_0=(|z|^2+\\sqrt T)^{1/2}$. Wherever this expression is nonzero, $$T\\le-t\\le T+b_0^4,\n \\qquad b_0^2\\le |z|^2+\\sqrt{-t}\\le(1+\\sqrt2)b_0^2.$$ The ancient scale is therefore comparable to $b_0$. The argument of $\\psi$ has bounded derivatives in spatial units $b_0$ and temporal units $b_0^4$ on its transition region. The trace norm, Hölder multiplication, and scale comparability prove the required estimate below $-T$. Across that face the values agree. Splitting a temporal increment at $-T$ bounds its $\\alpha/4$ Hölder quotient by the sum of the two one-sided bounds; a spatial increment is handled by the trace bound on comparable boxes. This proves the full parabolic $C^\\alpha$ bound. No time derivative of the source is required. The construction is real, linear, and radial. Its continuation below $-T$ is in fact supported in $-26\\beta^4\\le t<-T$, though a temporal support restriction is unnecessary for the ancient inverse. ◻",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        }
      ],

      "output": "A bounded real radial source on the ancient flat cylinder.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-glue-extension"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-glue-inverse",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "glue:parametrix",
          "exact_quote": "$$\\label{glue:parametrix}\n \\mathcal PD_{\\mathrm{app}}=I+\\mathcal R,\\qquad\n \\lVert D_{\\mathrm{app}}\\rVert_{Y_\\alpha\\to X_\\alpha}\\le C,\\qquad\n \\lVert \\mathcal R\\rVert_{Y_\\alpha\\to Y_\\alpha}\n \\le C\\bigl(\\beta^2+\\eta^{(1-\\alpha)/4}\\bigr).$$ Choose $\\beta_0$ and then $\\eta_0$ small enough that the last bound is at most $1/2$ throughout the asserted range, decreasing the earlier $\\eta_0$ if necessary. The convergent Neumann series defines $$\\label{glue:neumann}\n D=D_{\\mathrm{app}}(I+\\mathcal R)^{-1}.$$ It satisfies $\\mathcal PD=I$, is real and invariant, and has uniform norm at most twice the bound for $D_{\\mathrm{app}}$. This is a right inverse construction and requires no assertion of injectivity. ◻",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "glue:neumann",
          "exact_quote": "Define $$D_{\\mathrm{app}}f=V_i+V_o,\\qquad \\mathcal R=R_i+J_o.$$ Equations <a href=\"#glue:inner-identity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:inner-identity\">[glue:inner-identity]</a>, <a href=\"#glue:outer-source\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:outer-source\">[glue:outer-source]</a>, and <a href=\"#glue:outer-identity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:outer-identity\">[glue:outer-identity]</a> give the exact operator identity $$\\label{glue:parametrix}\n \\mathcal PD_{\\mathrm{app}}=I+\\mathcal R,\\qquad\n \\lVert D_{\\mathrm{app}}\\rVert_{Y_\\alpha\\to X_\\alpha}\\le C,\\qquad\n \\lVert \\mathcal R\\rVert_{Y_\\alpha\\to Y_\\alpha}\n \\le C\\bigl(\\beta^2+\\eta^{(1-\\alpha)/4}\\bigr).$$ Choose $\\beta_0$ and then $\\eta_0$ small enough that the last bound is at most $1/2$ throughout the asserted range, decreasing the earlier $\\eta_0$ if necessary. The convergent Neumann series defines $$\\label{glue:neumann}\n D=D_{\\mathrm{app}}(I+\\mathcal R)^{-1}.$$ It satisfies $\\mathcal PD=I$, is real and invariant, and has uniform norm at most twice the bound for $D_{\\mathrm{app}}$. This is a right inverse construction and requires no assertion of injectivity. ◻",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"

      "id": "step-compact-parametrix-and-inverse",

      "method": "Cancel the inner cutoff commutator in the outer forcing and invert the remaining small geometric and outer commutator errors by a Neumann series.",

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-glue-special-error",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-compact-parametrix-and-inverse",
        "step-short-time-spatial-interpolation",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "glue:app-special-small",
          "exact_quote": "The extra regularity in Lemma <a href=\"#glue:background\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"glue:background\">20</a> gives $$\\label{glue:outer-error-high}\n \\lVert f_e\\rVert_{Y_{\\alpha'}(s_\\beta)}\\le C.$$ The constant is uniform in $\\beta,T$: in the transition annulus all cutoff derivatives have bounded scaled size, and elsewhere this is <a href=\"#glue:error-weight\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:error-weight\">[glue:error-weight]</a> or the fixed exterior smooth bound. Apply Lemma <a href=\"#ana:short\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ana:short\">15</a> at exponent $\\alpha'$ to the outer zero-data problem with source $f_e$. The coefficient bounds hold at that exponent, so its solution has uniformly bounded $X_{\\alpha'}(s_\\beta)$ norm. Equation <a href=\"#ana:fourth-small\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:fourth-small\">[ana:fourth-small]</a> of Lemma <a href=\"#ana:interpolation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ana:interpolation\">16</a> gives an $X'_\\alpha(s_\\beta)$ bound of $C\\eta^{(\\alpha'-\\alpha)/4}$. Multiplication by $\\chi_o$ is bounded in these scaled spaces, and $s_\\beta$ is comparable to $\\sigma$ on the output support. This proves the first estimate below; the commutator bound <a href=\"#glue:outer-error-small\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"glue:outer-error-small\">[glue:outer-error-small]</a> proves the second: $$\\label{glue:app-special-small}\n \\lVert D_{\\mathrm{app}}f_e\\rVert_{X'_\\alpha}\n \\le C\\eta^{(\\alpha'-\\alpha)/4},\\qquad\n \\lVert \\mathcal Rf_e\\rVert_{Y_\\alpha}\n \\le C\\eta^{(1-\\alpha)/4}.$$ The first estimate includes the temporal Hölder seminorms of all spatial derivatives through order four, as supplied by <a href=\"#ana:fourth-small\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ana:fourth-small\">[ana:fourth-small]</a>. It makes no claim that the first time derivative is small: the outer zero-data solution has time derivative $f_e$ at the initial face.",
          "source_id": "PM-352.1"
        },

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-real-root-isolation**: The comparison polynomial has exactly one real root, strictly between the stated rational endpoints. The executable check certifies this finite algebraic component of the profile construction.
- **invariant-strict-monotonicity**: The derivative discriminant is negative and the leading coefficient is positive, so the comparison polynomial is strictly increasing on the real line.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-finite-check-as-global-proof**: Treat acceptance of the root calculation as certification of the shrinking profile or the finite-time geometric singularity. -> Root isolation does not establish the shooting inequalities, half-line matching, ancient inverse, or compact nonlinear correction.
- **anti-floating-point-root-signs**: Replace the endpoint signs by rounded numerical root estimates. -> Rounded estimates do not certify the strict signs needed to isolate the root.
- **anti-real-root-as-complex-bounds**: Use the real-root certificate as a certificate of every spectral and convolution estimate. -> The nonreal root boxes, projection bounds, and convolution constants require additional calculations.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The real-root isolation argument is a finite exact rational calculation. This implementation covers that component only. The global profile matching invokes Brouwer’s fixed point theorem, and the geometric existence result is outside this executable certificate.

```python
# INPUTS: A JSON object with rational-string endpoints left and right.
# PRECONDITIONS: Both strings denote finite rational numbers and left < right.
# OUTPUTS: Exact rational derivative gap and endpoint values, plus a strict isolation flag.
# TIME: A fixed collection of exact rational arithmetic operations; integer costs depend on input size.
# SPACE: Storage for the input rationals and their exact intermediate values.
# ARITHMETIC: Arbitrary-precision integers and exact rational arithmetic.
# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an exception; a non-isolating interval returns certified false.
import json
import sys
from fractions import Fraction as F

def run(data):
    left = F(data['left'])
    right = F(data['right'])
    if left >= right:
        raise ValueError('Endpoints must be strictly ordered')
    a = F(15,56)
    b = F(751,8*56**2)
    c = 1-F(1215,8*56**3)
    def polynomial(x):
        return ((x+a)*x+b)*x+c
    gap = a*a-3*b
    lv = polynomial(left)
    rv = polynomial(right)
    return {'derivative_gap':str(gap), 'left_value':str(lv), 'right_value':str(rv), 'certified':bool(gap < 0 and lv < 0 and rv > 0)}

print(json.dumps(run(json.load(sys.stdin)), sort_keys=True))
```

    "oracle_code": "import json\nimport sys\nimport sympy as sp\n\ntry:\n    with open(sys.argv[1], encoding='utf-8') as handle:\n        data = json.load(handle)\n    with open(sys.argv[2], encoding='utf-8') as handle:\n        result = json.load(handle)\n    expected = json.loads(sys.argv[3])\n    if set(result) != {'derivative_gap','left_value','right_value','certified'}:\n        raise ValueError('Unexpected output fields')\n    if set(expected) != {'certified'} or type(expected['certified']) is not bool:\n        raise ValueError('Invalid expected contract')\n    left = sp.Rational(data['left'])\n    right = sp.Rational(data['right'])\n    if not left < right:\n        raise ValueError('Unordered endpoints')\n    x = sp.Symbol('x')\n    denominator = 8*56**3\n    integer_poly = sp.Poly(denominator*x**3 + 120*56**2*x**2 + 751*56*x + denominator-1215, x, domain=sp.QQ)\n    derivative = integer_poly.diff()\n    normalized_gap = sp.discriminant(derivative.as_expr(),x)/(4*denominator**2)\n    left_value = integer_poly.eval(left)/denominator\n    right_value = integer_poly.eval(right)/denominator\n    for key, value in [('derivative_gap',normalized_gap),('left_value',left_value),('right_value',right_value)]:\n        if not isinstance(result[key],str) or sp.Rational(result[key]) != value:\n            raise ValueError('Incorrect exact arithmetic result')\n    global_count = integer_poly.count_roots(-sp.oo,sp.oo)\n    interval_count = integer_poly.count_roots(left,right)\n    certificate = bool(normalized_gap < 0 and global_count == 1 and interval_count == 1 and left_value < 0 and right_value > 0)\n    if type(result['certified']) is not bool:\n        raise ValueError('Flag must be Boolean')\n    if result['certified'] != certificate or expected['certified'] != certificate:\n        raise ValueError('Incorrect isolation verdict')\nexcept Exception:\n    sys.exit(1)\nsys.exit(0)\n",
    "output_contract": "A JSON object with exactly derivative_gap, left_value, right_value, and certified. Rational values must equal the exact derivative gap and endpoint evaluations. The flag is true precisely when the derivative gap is negative and the endpoint values have strictly opposite signs in increasing order.",
    "reason": "The real-root isolation argument is a finite exact rational calculation. This implementation covers that component only. The global profile matching invokes Brouwer’s fixed point theorem, and the geometric existence result is outside this executable certificate.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: A JSON object with rational-string endpoints left and right.\n# PRECONDITIONS: Both strings denote finite rational numbers and left < right.\n# OUTPUTS: Exact rational derivative gap and endpoint values, plus a strict isolation flag.\n# TIME: A fixed collection of exact rational arithmetic operations; integer costs depend on input size.\n# SPACE: Storage for the input rationals and their exact intermediate values.\n# ARITHMETIC: Arbitrary-precision integers and exact rational arithmetic.\n# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an exception; a non-isolating interval returns certified false.\nimport json\nimport sys\nfrom fractions import Fraction as F\n\ndef run(data):\n    left = F(data['left'])\n    right = F(data['right'])\n    if left >= right:\n        raise ValueError('Endpoints must be strictly ordered')\n    a = F(15,56)\n    b = F(751,8*56**2)\n    c = 1-F(1215,8*56**3)\n    def polynomial(x):\n        return ((x+a)*x+b)*x+c\n    gap = a*a-3*b\n    lv = polynomial(left)\n    rv = polynomial(right)\n    return {'derivative_gap':str(gap), 'left_value':str(lv), 'right_value':str(rv), 'certified':bool(gap < 0 and lv < 0 and rv > 0)}\n\nprint(json.dumps(run(json.load(sys.stdin)), sort_keys=True))\n",
    "test_vectors": [
      {
        "expected": "{\"certified\":true}",
        "input": "{\"left\":\"-1.08659\",\"right\":\"-1.08655\"}"
      },
      {
        "expected": "{\"certified\":true}",
        "input": "{\"left\":\"-2\",\"right\":\"0\"}"
      },
      {
        "expected": "{\"certified\":false}",
        "input": "{\"left\":\"-1\",\"right\":\"0\"}"
      },
      {
        "expected": "{\"certified\":false}",
        "input": "{\"left\":\"0\",\"right\":\"1\"}"
      }
    ]
  },
  "pm_id": "PM-352.1",

}

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The integer interval construction supports software for rigorous arithmetic enclosures. Multiplication encloses all endpoint products, and reciprocal evaluation excludes intervals containing zero. This transfers the arithmetic component; it does not supply an executable construction of the singular metric. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The compact realization constructs an invariant geometric flow through analytic existence arguments. It supplies neither a simulation discretization nor a rendering or interaction construction. |
| tools | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The midpoint construction and its explicit error allowance support enclosure inspection tools that distinguish selecting a representative from enclosing a solution. The source charges midpoint displacement separately rather than treating the selected point as an interval certificate. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Validated shooting has a concrete finite construction: propagate Taylor enclosures and parameter variations, account for rounded reference jumps and transfer errors, then bound the nonlinear remainder before projecting the endpoint jet. Adapting it requires the analytic enclosure and positivity arguments as well as the finite arithmetic. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The paper gives a concrete representation for certified interval data using integer endpoint pairs and a shared denominator. Its closure operations preserve enclosures through outward rounding, while representative selection has a separate contract. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The transfer estimates control mathematical errors between exact and rounded matrices. They do not specify a hardware execution layout, parallel arithmetic design, or accelerator resource model. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite shooting proposition certifies positivity and matching coordinates for an ordinary differential equation. These are analytic correctness properties, with no cryptographic primitive, adversary model, or security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The ancient inverse acts on weighted radial parabolic function spaces and selects initial values through its construction. The theorem provides no learning objective, training procedure, statistical model, or generalization guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The profile matching step obtains an existence point from continuity and Brouwer's theorem. It supplies no finite optimization algorithm, objective function, stopping rule, or complexity bound for locating that point. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The two-sided recursion is an analytic series for an evolution equation. Its future-forcing selection is permitted because initial values are free; it is not a causal distributed protocol or a consistency construction. |
| other (Validated numerical analysis) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The analytic continuation argument supplies a concrete Taylor enclosure construction for the reference states and variations. Complex-disk bounds control truncation error, and the nonlinear bootstrap extends the finite calculation to the full shooting family. This is adaptable to rigorously enclosed boundary-value matching problems with corresponding denominator and continuation bounds. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-existential-initial-data-and-dimension** (counterexample_scope): The singularity theorem constructs particular invariant initial metrics on the stated projective space. Its product extension does not classify arbitrary initial metrics or establish singularities in every complex dimension. Blocked use: ['Claim that every smooth initial metric develops a singularity.', 'Extend the construction to dimensions outside the stated product range.']
- **limit-stationary-product-factor** (hypothesis): The product construction requires a compact connected Kähler factor with constant scalar curvature. The stationary-factor argument does not establish the same product solution for an arbitrary evolving factor. Blocked use: ['Apply the stationary product formula to a factor with nonconstant scalar curvature.']
- **limit-smooth-versus-weak-continuation** (formal_scope): Finite-time failure of smooth continuation does not refute global weak continuation or convergence in the finite-energy potential setting described in the introduction. Blocked use: ['Reject weak Calabi flows on the basis of the smooth singularity.', 'Interpret weak convergence as smooth continuation through the singular time.']
- **limit-positive-smooth-momentum-reconstruction** (hypothesis): Metric reconstruction requires a smooth momentum coefficient and a strictly positive multiplier. Solving the scalar equation without these geometric conditions does not establish a smooth Kähler flow. Blocked use: ['Reconstruct a Kähler metric from a coefficient whose positivity multiplier vanishes.', 'Ignore endpoint smoothness in the momentum interval.']
- **limit-legendre-affine-normalization** (hypothesis): The affine part of the Legendre potential must follow the prescribed evolution. Arbitrary time-dependent affine changes can alter the exact normalized potential equation. Blocked use: ['Treat arbitrary time-dependent projective dilations as preserving the stated potential normalization without an additional term.']
- **limit-momentum-bound-not-metric-equivalence** (formal_scope): Uniform bounds on the momentum positivity multiplier do not imply uniform equivalence to the Fubini–Study metric because the relation to fixed affine coordinates varies. Blocked use: ['Infer uniform metric equivalence or bounded curvature from the positivity multiplier alone.']
- **limit-curvature-obstruction-requires-subleading-correction** (hypothesis): The curvature obstruction requires a nonzero profile derivative at the origin, a smooth positive compact solution, and a correction with the stated strictly lower-order origin bound. Without these conditions the leading curvature divergence need not follow. Blocked use: ['Deduce curvature blow-up from the flat profile without constructing the compact correction.', 'Allow a correction of the leading singular order and assume it cannot cancel the profile.']
- **limit-certified-shooting-parameter-regime** (hypothesis): The finite shooting bounds concern the specified affine parameter square and origin family. Their uniform positivity and coordinate estimates do not automatically extend to nearby parameter regions outside that square. Blocked use: ['Reuse the matching tolerances for an enlarged or differently centered shooting family.', 'Replace the whole-family estimate with a central trajectory calculation.']
- **limit-interval-rounding-and-midpoint-selection** (approximation): Interval arithmetic must round outward, reciprocal evaluation requires exclusion of zero, and midpoint selection incurs a separate error. A representative point is not an enclosure. Blocked use: ['Use a reciprocal interval across zero.', 'Replace an enclosing interval by its midpoint without charging displacement error.', 'Substitute ordinary rounded arithmetic for the enclosure operations.']
- **limit-taylor-enclosures-require-analytic-continuation** (hypothesis): Taylor truncation allowances rely on holomorphic continuation, bounded state and tangent entries, and denominators separated from zero on the complex disk. Finite coefficient propagation alone does not justify the analytic enclosure. Blocked use: ['Certify a trajectory from truncated Taylor coefficients without a remainder bound.', 'Apply the disk argument after a denominator approaches zero.']
- **limit-transfer-and-nonlinear-error-accounting** (hypothesis): The shooting certificate requires physical derivative conversion, reference jump allowances, transfer perturbation control, and a nonlinear continuation bootstrap. First variations alone do not certify the entire nonlinear parameter family. Blocked use: ['Apply scaled transfer errors directly as physical derivative errors.', 'Omit jumps between rounded reference trajectories.', 'Infer nonlinear continuation solely from linear parameter predictions.']
- **limit-tail-contraction-domain** (hypothesis): The half-line tail construction uses the specified profile parameter range and bounded real stable coordinate. It supplies a bounded family suitable for matching, not an unrestricted tail solver for arbitrary endpoint jets. Blocked use: ['Prescribe arbitrary growing-mode coordinates at the matching point.', 'Apply the contraction bounds outside their source ball or parameter range.']
- **limit-topological-matching-not-finite-selection** (nonconstructive): Continuity and Brouwer matching establish existence of a matching parameter. The argument does not supply a finite certified procedure selecting that exact parameter. Blocked use: ['Present finite root isolation or finite shooting checks as an executable construction of the complete profile.', 'Claim a computable stopping rule for exact matching from the self-map argument alone.']
- **limit-asymptotic-expansion-not-convergent-series** (approximation): The differentiated expansion is an asymptotic statement at each fixed order. Convergence of the infinite formal series is not asserted, and the late starting point and constants may depend on the chosen orders. Blocked use: ['Evaluate an infinite convergent series as though convergence were proved.', 'Use order-independent remainder constants without a separate argument.']
- **limit-local-estimate-coefficients-and-time-buffer** (hypothesis): The parabolic estimates require positive ellipticity, controlled coefficients in the stated units, and the prescribed spatial and preceding-time gaps. Their lack of terminal data does not remove those requirements. Blocked use: ['Apply the estimates to a degenerate principal symbol.', 'Discard the preceding-time buffer in the interior estimate.', 'Assume coefficient bounds in unscaled coordinates suffice for scale-uniform estimates.']
- **limit-short-time-estimate-and-exponent-gap** (hypothesis): The forward estimates require zero initial data and a short interval relative to the minimum spatial scale. Smallness through the highest spatial derivatives requires a strict source Hölder exponent gap and omits the time derivative. Blocked use: ['Apply the zero-data smallness estimate to arbitrary initial data.', 'Claim that shortening time makes the full time-derivative norm small.', 'Remove the exponent gap from the highest-derivative smallness estimate.']
- **limit-ancient-inverse-selects-initial-values** (formal_scope): The ancient inverse selects initial values and uses future forcing for the outside spectral components. It is not a causal forward inverse for arbitrary prescribed initial data or a profile stability theorem. Blocked use: ['Use the ancient inverse as an online initial-value solution operator.', 'Infer profile stability or nondegeneracy from existence of the right inverse.']
- **limit-selected-spectral-weight-and-annular-integrability** (nonconstructive): The spectral weight is selected after a discreteness argument rather than prescribed arbitrarily. Its positive lower endpoint is essential for the annular spacetime integrability used to recover the full weighted estimate. Blocked use: ['Set the correction weight at its excluded endpoint.', 'Assume an arbitrary prescribed weight avoids the spectrum.', 'Treat the spectral selection as a computed spectral enclosure.']
- **limit-exact-cutoff-cancellation-and-small-parameters** (hypothesis): The compact inverse requires nested cutoff supports, exact cancellation of the inner commutator in the outer forcing, and sufficiently small localization and elapsed-time parameters. The inner commutator is bounded but need not be small. Blocked use: ['Discard the inner commutator as a small error.', 'Alter cutoff supports without rechecking exact cancellation and coefficient agreement.', 'Interpret a right inverse as proving injectivity.']
- **limit-specific-residual-and-nonlinear-contraction** (hypothesis): The nonlinear correction uses smallness of the inverse applied to the particular background residual in the primed spatial norm. It does not require a small inverse operator norm, and the time derivative is recovered from the fixed-point identity afterward. Blocked use: ['Assume the inverse is small on arbitrary sources.', 'Perform the contraction while claiming unproved time-derivative smallness.', 'Choose nonlinear radius and localization parameters without preserving the established inverse bound and positivity margin.']
- **limit-missing-executable-certificate-body** (unknown): The supplied corpus introduces the complete exact-arithmetic certificate but provides an empty displayed program. Independent execution of the complete finite shooting certificate cannot be established from this supplied body. Blocked use: ['Claim an independently executed complete shooting certificate from the supplied program display.', 'Treat the finite real-root component as execution of the full shooting construction.']

          "anchor": "stmt-geo-reduction",
          "quote": "Let $I$ be an open time interval, and let $H\\in C^\\infty(I\\times[0,1])$ satisfy $$\\label{geo:positivity}\n b(t,x):=1+v_*(x)H(t,x)>0.$$ Define $$\\label{geo:momentum}\n v=v_*+v_*^2H=v_*b,\\qquad\n K(H)=w^{-1}(wv_*^2H)'',\\qquad\n \\mathcal AH=\\big[w^{-1}(wv_*^2H)''\\big]''.$$ At each time these data, together with an affine choice in the Legendre potential, determine a smooth $U(n)$-invariant Kähler form in $[\\omega_*]$. Its scalar curvature and average scalar curvature are $$\\label{geo:scalar}\n S(t,x)=n(n+1)-K(H)(t,x),\\qquad \\bar S=n(n+1).$$ If, in addition, $$\\label{geo:equation}\n H_t+(1+v_*H)^2\\mathcal AH=0,$$ then for any $t_0\\in I$ and any initial affine choice there is a smooth family of these forms satisfying the Calabi flow exactly. More precisely, its global potential relative to $\\omega(t_0)$ satisfies $$\\label{geo:potential-flow}\n \\phi(t_0)=0,\\qquad\n \\omega(t)=\\omega(t_0)+i\\partial\\bar\\partial\\phi(t),\\qquad\n \\phi_t=S(\\omega(t))-\\bar S.$$",
          "quote_sha256": "b26ea93682a33ee5ef476f9a0b0e5af2a4ba38cb17570b161a7764717fcc5d8e"
        }
      ],

          "quote": "#### Matching the two solutions\n\nFor a parameter $z_*\\in\\mathcal Z$, apply Lemma <a href=\"#prof:tail\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prof:tail\">10</a> using the value $d(z_*)$ and the coordinate $q_{\\rm shot}(z_*)$ supplied by the finite shot.
